Stochastic Control/Stopping Problem with Expectation Constraints
Erhan Bayraktar, Song Yao

Abstract
We study a stochastic control/stopping problem with a series of inequality-type and equality-type expectation constraints in a general non-Markovian framework. We demonstrate that the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints (CSEC) is independent of a specific probability setting and is equivalent to the constrained stochastic control/stopping problem in weak formulation (an optimization over joint laws of Brownian motion, state dynamics, diffusion controls and stopping rules on an enlarged canonical space). Using a martingale-problem formulation of controlled SDEs in spirit of [ 39 ] , we characterize the probability classes in weak formulation by countably many actions of canonical processes, and thus obtain the upper semi-analyticity of the CSEC value function. Then we employ a measurable selection argument to establish a dynamic programming principle (DPP) in weak formulation for the CSEC value function, in which the conditional expected costs act as additional states for constraint levels at the intermediate horizon. This article extends [ 18 ] to the expectation-constraint case. We extend our previous work [ 4 ] to the more complicated setting where the diffusion is controlled. Compared to that paper the topological properties of diffusion-control spaces and the corresponding measurability are more technically involved which complicate the arguments especially for the measurable selection for the super-solution side of DPP in the weak formulation. MSC: 93E20, 60G40, 49L20, 60G44 Keywords: Stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints, martingale-problem formulation, enlarged canonical space, Polish space of diffusion controls, Polish space of stopping times, dynamic programming principle, regular conditional probability distribution, measurable selection.



1
Introduction

In this paper, we analyze a continuous-time stochastic control/stopping problem with
a series of inequality-type and equality-type expectation constraints in a general non-Markovian framework.

Let the game start from time
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
with a historical path of state
{\bf x}|_{[0,t]}
.
The player can choose an open-loop control
\mu\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

to make the state of the game evolve according to some controlled SDE on a probability space
({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p})

whose drift and diffusion coefficients depend on the past trajectories of the solution.
Let
{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
denote this controlled state process.
The player also decides an exercise time
\tau
to maximize the expectation of her accumulative reward
\int_{t}^{\tau}\negthinspace f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\,dr
plus her terminal reward
\pi\big(\tau,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\tau\land\cdot}\big)

while she is subject to a series of constraints: for
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,
the expectation of some accumulative cost
\int_{t}^{\tau}\negthinspace g_{i}\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr
should not exceed certain level
y_{i}
and the expectation of some other accumulative cost
\int_{t}^{\tau}\negthinspace h_{i}\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr
should exactly reach certain level
z_{i}
.
This stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints (SCEC for short)
has many applications in economy, engineering, finance, management, etc.

Let
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
be the SCEC value with
(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\{y_{i}\},\{z_{i}\}\big)
.
We aim to establish an associated dynamic programming principle (DPP) of this value function without imposing any regularity condition on reward/cost functions in time, state and control variables. A dynamic programming principle of a stochastic optimization problem allows one to maximize/minimize the problem stage by stage
in a backward recursive way. It entails the problem value function to be measurable so that one can do optimization first at an intermediate horizon.

To obtain the measurability of the SCEC value function,
we first study the topological and measurable properties of the path space
\mathbb{J}
of diffusion control
\mu
:
More precisely, we show that
\mathbb{J}
is a Borel space under a weak topology and attain a representation of its Borel sigma-field
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
(Lemma
1.2
and Lemma
1.3
).
Inspired by
[
16
,
18
]
, we then embed diffusion control
\mu
and stopping rule
\tau
together with the Brownian and state information into an enlarged canonical space
{\overline{\Omega}}
and regard their joint distribution as a new type of controls.
The optimization of the total expected reward over constrained diffusion controls/stopping times transforms into
a maximal expectation of reward functional over a class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
of probability measures on
{\overline{\Omega}}
under which four canonical coordinates
({\overline{W}},{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}},{\overline{T}})
serve as Brownian motion, diffusion control, state process and stopping rules respectively.
We demonstrate that such a transformation is equivalent (Theorem
3.1
), namely,
the value
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
of SCEC in strong formulation (i.e., on
{\cal Q}
) is equal to the value
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
of SCEC in weak formulation (i.e., over
{\overline{\Omega}}
). Hence, the SCEC value is a robust value, independent of a specific probability model.

For the measurability of SCEC value functions,
we next take advantage of the martingale-problem formulation from
[
39
]
to
describe the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
as a series of probabilistic tests on stochastic behaviors of the canonical coordinates of
{\overline{\Omega}}
.
With such a countable characterization,
we employ a Polish space of diffusion control processes (Lemma
4.1
) and a Polish space of stopping times constructed in
[
4
]

to deduce that the set-valued mapping
(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
has Borel-measurable graph
and the SCEC value function
V\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{V}}
is thus upper semi-analytic in
(t,{\bf x},y,z)
, (Theorem
4.1
).

Our main achievement is to derive a DPP for
{\overline{V}}
in weak formulation, which takes conditional expectations of the remaining costs as additional states for constraint levels at the intermediate horizon (Theorem
5.1
).
For the subsolution side of this DPP, we use the regular conditional probability distribution
to show that the probability classes
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
,
\,\forall\,(t,{\bf x},y,z)
are stable under conditioning.

For the supersolution side of the DPP, we exploit a measurable selection theorem in the analytic-set theory
to paste a class of locally
\varepsilon-
optimal probability measures.
We make a delicate analysis to demonstrate that the second canonical coordinate
{\overline{U}}
serves as a constrained diffusion control under the pasted probability measure,
and we apply the martingale-problem formulation again to indicate that the canonical coordinates
({\overline{W}},{\overline{X}})
are still Brownian motion and the state process under the pasted probability measure. Similar to the arguments in
[
4
]
, the fourth canonical coordinate
{\overline{T}}
is a constrained stopping time under the pasted probability measure.
To wit, the probability classes
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
’s are also stable under concatenation.

Relevant Literature.

Kennedy
[
22
]
employed a
Lagrange multiplier
method
to reformulate a discrete-time optimal stopping problem with first-moment constraint as a minimax problem
and showed that the optimal value of the dual problem
is equal to that of the primal problem.
The Lagrangian technique was later adopted in many economic/financial applications of
optimal stopping problems with expectation constraints, see e.g.
[
31
,
23
,
19
,
2
,
42
,
24
,
29
,
28
,
41
]
.
Pfeiffer et al.
[
30
]
recently took a Lagrange relaxation approach
to obtain a duality result for general stochastic control problems with expectation constraints.

In their study of a continuous-time stochastic optimization problem of controlled Markov processes,
El Karoui, Huu Nguyen and Jeanblanc-Picqué
[
16
]

viewed joint laws of state and control processes
as
control rules
on the product space of canonical state space and control space.
They utilized a measurable selection theorem in the analytic-set theory to establish a DPP
without assuming any regularity on the reward functional.
Nutz et al.
[
27
,
26
]
came up with a similar idea
to analyze a superheging problem under volatility uncertainty.
They modeled the “uncertainty” by path-dependent classes of controlled-diffusion laws and explored the analytic measurability of these classes. Using the measurable selection techniques,
the authors obtained DPP result in a form of time-consistency of a sub-linear expectation and they thus established a duality formula for the robust superhedging of measurable claims.
The approach of
[
27
,
26
]
was later developed by e.g.
[
32
,
33
]
to derive DPPs of various non-Markovian control problems.
Yu et al.
[
15
]
took a similar measurable selection argument to analyze
the DPP of a stochastic control problem with certain expectation constraint in which they dynamically relaxed the expectation constraint by a family of auxiliary supermartingales.

El Karoui and Tan
[
17
,
18
]
used the measurable selection argument to attain the DPP for a general stochastic control/stopping problem by embedding diffusion controls and stopping times into an enlarged canonical space in the spirit of
[
16
]
. However, the probability class they considered in weak formulation is not suitable for stochastic control/stopping with expectation constraints, see our discussion in Remark
3.4
.
Instead, we additional require in (D4) of Definition
3.1
that under each
{\overline{P}}
of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)

the time canonical coordinate
{\overline{T}}
acts as some stopping time
(it turns out that such a restriction does not affect the unconstrained stochastic control/stopping problem in weak formulation).
By constructing a Polish space of diffusion control processes and utilizing a Polish space of stopping times from
[
4
]
, we manage to derive the Borel measurability of graph
{\big[\negthinspace\big[\,{\overline{{\cal P}}}\,\big]\negthinspace\big]}
and thus obtain the measurability of the SCEC value functions.
Because of condition (D4) and expectation constraints, it is more technically involved to
verify the stability of our probability classes
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
under conditioning and concatenation
and thus establish a DPP for the SCEC value function
{\overline{V}}
.

As to the optimal stopping problems with expectation constraints, Ankirchner et al.
[
1
]
and Miller
[
25
]
took different approaches by transforming the constrained optimal stopping problems for diffusion processes to stochastic optimization problems with martingale controls.
The former characterizes the value function in terms of a Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman equation and obtains a verification theorem, while the latter embeds the optimal stopping problem with first-moment constraint into a time-inconsistent (unconstrained) stopping problem. However, the authors only postulate dynamic programming principles for their corresponding problems.
In contrast, our previous work
[
4
]
exploited a measurable selection method to rigorously establish
a dynamic programming principle for the optimal stopping problem with expectation constraints.

An interesting related topic to our research is optimal stopping with constraint on the distribution of stopping times.
Bayraktar and Miller
[
3
]

studied the problem of optimally stopping a Brownian motion with the restriction that
the distribution of the stopping time must equal a given measure with finitely many atoms,
and obtained a dynamic programming result which relates each of the sequential optimal control problems.
Källblad
[
20
]
used measure-valued martingales to transform
the distribution-constrained optimal stopping problem to a stochastic
control problem and derived a DPP by measurable selection arguments.
From the perspective of optimal transport, Beiglböck et al.
[
5
]
gave a geometric interpretation of optimal stopping times of a Brownian motion with distribution constraint.

Moreover, for stochastic control problems with state constraints, stochastic target problems with controlled losses and related geometric DPP, see
[
10
,
11
,
13
,
35
,
36
,
37
,
14
,
8
,
12
,
9
]
.

The rest of the paper is organized as follows:
Section
2
introduces the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints
in a generic probabilistic setting.
Section
3
shows that the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints
can be equivalently embedded into an enlarged canonical space: i.e.,
the SCEC in strong formulation has the same value as the SCEC in weak formulation.
In Section
4
, we use the martingale-problem formulation to make a countable characterization of the probability class in weak formulation. With such a characterization,
we employ a Polish space of diffusion control processes and a Polish space of stopping times to demonstrate that the SCEC value function
is upper semi-analytic. Then in Section
5
, we utilize a measurable selection argument to establish a dynamic programming principle in weak formulation for the SCEC value function. We defer the proofs of our results to Section
6
and put some technical lemmata in the appendix.

We close this section by a description of our notation and a review of the martingale-problem formulation of controlled SDEs.

1.1
Notation and Preliminaries

Throughout this paper, let us denote
a^{+}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace a\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace 0
and
a^{-}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(-a)\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace 0
for any
a\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
.
We set
\mathbb{Q}_{+}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}\cap[0,\infty)
,
\mathbb{Q}^{2,<}_{+}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\\
\big\{(s,r)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}_{+}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}_{+}\negthinspace:s\negthinspace<\negthinspace r\big\}

and set
\Re\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(-\infty,\infty]^{\mathbb{N}}
as the product of countably many copies of
(-\infty,\infty]
.
On
\mathbb{T}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace[0,\infty]
we define a metric
\rho_{\overset{}{+}}(t_{1},t_{2})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big|\arctan(t_{1})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\arctan(t_{2})\big|
,
\,\forall\,t_{1},t_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{T}

and consider the induced topology by
\rho_{\overset{}{+}}
.

For a generic topological space
\big(\mathbb{X},\mathfrak{T}(\mathbb{X})\big)
,
denote its Borel sigma-field by
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{X})
. We let
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{X})
be the set of all probability measures on
\big(\mathbb{X},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{X})\big)

and equip
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{X})
with the topology of weak convergence
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{X})\big)
.
By e.g. Corollary 7.25.1 of
[
6
]
,
\big(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{X}),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{X}))\big)

is a
Borel space
(i.e., homeomorphic to a Borel subset of a complete separable metric space).

Let
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. For any
x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\delta\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
, let
O_{\delta}(x)
denote
the open ball centered at
x
with radius
\delta
and let
\overline{O}_{\delta}(x)
be its closure.
For any
x,\widetilde{x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{n}

we denote the usual inner product by
x\cdot\widetilde{x}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}\widetilde{x}_{i}
, and for any
n\negthinspace\times\negthinspace n-
real matrices
A,\widetilde{A}

we denote the Frobenius inner product by
A\negthinspace:\negthinspace\widetilde{A}:=trace\big(A\widetilde{A}^{T}\big)
, where
\widetilde{A}^{T}
is the transpose of
\widetilde{A}
.
Let
\big\{{\cal E}^{n}_{i}\big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable subbase of the Euclidean topology
\mathfrak{T}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Then
\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{\underset{i=1}{\overset{n}{\cap}}{\cal E}^{n}_{k_{i}}\negthinspace:\{k_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\Big\}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\{\emptyset,\mathbb{R}^{n}\}

forms a countable base of
\mathfrak{T}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and thus
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big(\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\big)
.
We also set
\widehat{\mathscr{O}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\cup}}\big(\mathbb{Q}_{+}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\big)^{k}
.
For any
\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
let
D\varphi
be its gradient,
D^{2}\varphi
be its Hessian matrix and denote
D^{0}\varphi\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi
.
For
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,n
, define
\varphi_{i}(x)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace x_{i}
,
\,\forall\,x\negthinspace=\negthinspace(x_{1},\cdots\negthinspace,x_{n})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
We let
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
collect these coordinate functions and their products, i.e.,

\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\{\varphi_{i}\}^{n}_{i=1}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}\}^{n}_{i,j=1}
.

Let
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
be a generic probability space. For subsets
A_{1},A_{2}
of
\Omega
, we denote
A_{1}\Delta A_{2}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(A_{1}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace A^{c}_{2})\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace(A_{2}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace A^{c}_{1})
.
For a random variable
\xi
on
\Omega
with values in a measurable space
({\cal Q},{\cal G})
,
we say
\xi
is
{\cal F}/{\cal G}-
measurable if its induced sigma-field
\xi^{-1}({\cal G})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\{\xi^{-1}({\cal A})\negthinspace:\,\forall\,{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}\}
is
included in
{\cal F}
.
For a sub-sigma-field
\mathfrak{F}
of
{\cal F}
,
define
\mathscr{N}_{P}(\mathfrak{F})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal N}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Omega\negthinspace:{\cal N}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace A
for some
A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}\hbox{ with }P(A)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\big\}
,
which collects all
P-
null sets with respect to
\mathfrak{F}
.
For two sub-sigma-fields
\mathfrak{F}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{2}
of
{\cal F}
,
we denote
\mathfrak{F}_{1}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}_{2}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma(\mathfrak{F}_{1}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}_{2})
.
Let
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
.
For a filtration
{\bf F}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{{\cal F}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
of
{\cal F}
, we decree
{\cal F}_{t-}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal F}_{t}
and define
{\cal F}_{s-}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\Big(\underset{r\in[t,s)}{\cup}{\cal F}_{r}\Big)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)
; we also set
{\cal F}_{\infty}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\Big(\underset{s\in[t,\infty)}{\cup}{\cal F}_{s}\Big)

and refer to filtration
{\bf F}^{P}\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal F}^{P}_{s}\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}_{s}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}_{\infty})\big)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
as the
P-
augmentation of
{\bf F}
.
For a process
X\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{X_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
\Omega
with values in a topological space,
its raw filtration is
{\bf F}^{X}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal F}^{X}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma(X_{r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s])\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
.
We denote the
P-
augmentation of
{\bf F}^{X}
by
{\bf F}^{X,P}\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal F}^{X,P}_{s}\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}^{X}_{s}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{X}_{\infty})\big)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
and let
\mathscr{P}^{X}
be the
{\bf F}^{X}-
predictable sigma
-
field of
[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega
.
We call
X
a continuous process if its paths are all continuous.
When the time variable
s
of
X
has complicated form, we may write
X(s,\omega)
as
X_{s}(\omega)
for readability.
By default, a Brownian motion
\{B_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
is with respect to its raw filtration

{\bf F}^{B}
unless stated otherwise.

Fix
d,l\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. Let
\Omega_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})\negthinspace:\omega(0)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\big\}

be the space of all
\mathbb{R}^{d}-
valued continuous paths starting from
{\bf 0}
, which is a Polish space under the topology of locally uniform convergence.
Let
P_{0}
be the Wiener measure on
\big(\Omega_{0},\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\big)
, under which
the canonical process
W\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{W_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
of
\Omega_{0}
is a
d-
dimensional standard Brownian motion.
For any
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
W^{t}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace W_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace W_{t}
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
is also a Brownian motion on
\big(\Omega_{0},\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}),P_{0}\big)
.
Let
\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{l})
be the space of all
\mathbb{R}^{l}-
valued continuous paths endowed with the topology of locally uniform convergence.
The function
\mathfrak{l}_{1}(t,\omega_{0})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\omega_{0}(t\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\cdot)
is continuous in
(t,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}

while the function
\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(t\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\cdot)
is continuous in
(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
.

Let
\mathbb{U}
be a Polish space with a compatible metric
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}
and let
u_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
As a Polish space,
\mathbb{U}
is homeomorphic to a Borel subset
\mathfrak{E}
of
[0,1]
,
we denote this homeomorphism by
\mathscr{I}\negthinspace:\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathfrak{E}
.
Let
C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)

(
resp.
\widehat{C}_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
)
collect all real-valued bounded continuous (resp. bounded uniformly continuous) functions on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
For any
(\delta,\mathfrak{m},\phi)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
, set

O_{\delta}(\mathfrak{m},\phi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\negthinspace:\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace\delta\big\}
.

Lemma 1.1
.

There exist
\{\mathfrak{m}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
and

\{\phi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\widehat{C}_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
such that

\big\{O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\negthinspace:n,k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}

forms a countable subbase of
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
.

Let
\mathbb{J}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace L^{0}\big([0,\infty);\mathbb{U}\big)
denote the equivalence classes of
\mathbb{U}
-valued Borel measurable functions on
[0,\infty)

in the sense that
\mathfrak{u}_{1},\mathfrak{u}_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
are equivalent if
\mathfrak{u}_{1}(t)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{u}_{2}(t)
for a.e.
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
.
It will serve as the path space of
\mathbb{U}-
valued diffusion controls.
We can embed
\mathbb{J}
into
\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
via a mapping

\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\negthinspace:\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\ni\negthinspace\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace e^{-t}\delta_{\mathfrak{u}(t)}(du)dt\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
.
Let
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
be the topology induced by
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
via
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
. According to Lemma
1.1
,

\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
is generated by a countable subbase

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Big\{\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace:\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\phi_{j}(t,\mathfrak{u}(t))dt\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\mathfrak{m}_{k}(dt,du)\Big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace\frac{1}{n}\Big\},\quad\,\forall\,n,k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}.\quad

(1.1)

Lemma 1.2
.

\big(\mathbb{J},\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\big)
is a Borel space.

Let
L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)
collect all real
-
valued Borel-measurable functions on
(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
For any
\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)
, define

I_{\varphi}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi^{+}(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi^{-}(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds
,
\,\forall\,\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
.
The Borel sigma-field
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
of
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
can be generated by these random variables
I_{\varphi}
on
\mathbb{J}
.

Lemma 1.3
.

1
)
We have
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big(I_{\varphi};\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)\big)
.

2
)
Let
\psi\negthinspace:(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace[-\infty,\infty]
be a Borel-measurable function. Then
the mapping
\Psi(t,\mathfrak{s},\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}\psi\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}\psi^{+}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}\psi^{-}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\\
\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr
,
(t,\mathfrak{s},\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.

Let
b\negthinspace:(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{l}
and
\sigma\negthinspace:(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{l\times d}
be two Borel-measurable functions such that for any
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)

\displaystyle\big|b(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)\negthinspace-\negthinspace b(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime},u)\big|\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\sigma(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\sigma(t,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime},u)\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\kappa(t)\big\|\omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime}\big\|_{t},\quad\,\forall\,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}},~\,\forall\,u\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{U},\quad\qquad

(1.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\hbox{and}\quad\int_{0}^{t}\underset{u\in\mathbb{U}}{\sup}\,\big(|b(r,{\bf 0},u)|^{2}\negthinspace+\negthinspace|\sigma(r,{\bf 0},u)|^{2}\big)dr\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty,

(1.3)

where
\kappa\negthinspace:(0,\infty)\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace(0,\infty)
is some non-decreasing function and
\big\|\omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime}\big\|_{t}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{s\in[0,t]}{\sup}\,\big|\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(s)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{\prime}(s)\big|
.
Under conditions (
1.2
) and (
1.3
), controlled SDEs with coefficients
(b,\sigma)
are well-posed
(see e.g. Theorem V.7 of
[
34
]
):

Proposition 1.1
.

Let
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
be a probability space.
Given
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, let
\{B^{t}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a
d-
dimensional Brownian motion on
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)

and let
\mu\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
progressively measurable process.
For any
{\bf x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
, the SDE with the open-loop control
\mu

\displaystyle X_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(t)+\int_{t}^{s}b(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dB^{t}_{r},\;~\;\,\forall\,s\in[t,\infty)\;\hbox{ with initial condition }X\big|_{[0,t]}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}|_{[0,t]}

(1.4)

admits a unique strong solution
X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

(
i.e.,
X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}
is an
\{{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s\vee t}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process satisfying (
1.4
)
and
P\big\{X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widetilde{X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s},\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1

if
\big\{\widetilde{X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
is another
\{{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s\vee t}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process satisfying (
1.4
)
)
.

Let
\mathscr{H}_{o}
collect all
(-\infty,\infty]-
valued Borel-measurable functions
\psi
on
(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
such that
for any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}

and any
\mathbb{U}-
valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process
\mu^{o}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu^{o}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
, one has
E_{P_{0}}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{o}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
,
where
\{X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

is the unique strong solution of (
1.4
) on
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\Omega_{0},\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}),P_{0}\big)
with
(B^{t},\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(W^{t},\mu^{o}\big)
.

Moreover, we take the conventions
\inf\emptyset\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\infty
,
\sup\emptyset\negthinspace:=\negthinspace-\infty
and
(+\infty)\negthinspace+\negthinspace(-\infty)\negthinspace=\negthinspace-\infty
.
In particular, on a measure space
(\Omega,{\cal F},\mathfrak{m})
,
one can define the integral
\int_{\Omega}\xi\,d\mathfrak{m}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{\Omega}\xi^{+}\,d\mathfrak{m}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{\Omega}\xi^{-}\,d\mathfrak{m}
for any
[-\infty,\infty]-
valued
{\cal F}-
measurable random variable
\xi
on
\Omega
.

1.2
Review of Martingale-Problem Formulation of Controlled SDEs

In this subsection, we consider a general measurable space
(\Omega,{\cal F})
.
Let
\{B_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be an
\mathbb{R}^{d}-
valued continuous process on
\Omega
with
B_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 0}

and let
X\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{X_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be an
\mathbb{R}^{l}-
valued continuous process on
\Omega
such that

(B_{s},X_{s})
is
{\cal F}-
measurable for each
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
.

Given
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
, let
P
be a probability measure on
(\Omega,{\cal F})
such that

P\big\{X_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
.
We set
B^{t}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace B_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace B_{t}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

and let
\mu\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
progressively measurable process.
Define filtration
{\bf F}^{t}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{{\cal F}^{t}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
by
{\cal F}^{t}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{s}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace{\cal F}^{X}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma(B^{t}_{r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s])\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace\sigma(X_{r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,s])
and filtration
{\bf F}^{t,P}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{{\cal F}^{t,P}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
by
{\cal F}^{t,P}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace{\cal F}^{X}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{s}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}\big({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty}\big)\big)\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace{\cal F}^{X}_{s}
. For any
\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
, we define

\displaystyle\quad M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi\big(B^{t}_{s},X_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{b}(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi\big(B^{t}_{r},X_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi(B^{t}_{r},X_{r})dr,\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty),

where
\displaystyle\overline{b}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\binom{0}{b(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)}\negthinspace\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
,
\displaystyle\overline{\sigma}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\binom{I_{d\times d}}{\sigma(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)}\negthinspace\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{(d+l)\times d}
,
\,\forall\,(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
Clearly,
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is an
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
adapted continuous process
and it is even
{\bf F}^{t}-
adapted if the control process
\mu
is only
{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-
progressively measurable.
For any
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and
\mathfrak{a}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
, set
\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:|(B^{t}_{s},X_{s})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{a}|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)
, which is an
{\bf F}^{t}-
stopping time. In particular, we denote
\tau^{t}_{n}({\bf 0})
by
\tau^{t}_{n}
.

In virtue of
[
39
]
, we have the following martingale-problem formulation of controlled SDEs with coefficients
(b,\sigma)
on
\Omega
.

Proposition 1.2
.

Under the probabilistic setup of this subsection,
the process
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is bounded for any
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
and
the following statements are equivalent on
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
:

(
i
)
The process
B^{t}
is a Brownian motion and
P\{X_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s},\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\}=1
, where
\big\{X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

is the unique
\big\{{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process solving SDE (
1.4
).

(
ii
)

\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingale for any
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
.

(
iii
)

\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingale for any
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.

Moreover, if the control process
\mu
is
{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-
progressively measurable, the
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingales mentioned in
(
ii
)
and
(
iii
)
should be
{\bf F}^{t}-
martingales.

We have the following consequence of Proposition
1.2
.

Proposition 1.3
.

Let
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
be a probability space. Given
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
let
\{B_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be an
\mathbb{R}^{d}-
valued continuous process on
\Omega
with
B_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 0}
such that
the process
B^{t}

is a Brownian motion on
(\Omega,{\cal F},P)
,
and let
\mu\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
progressively measurable process.

Let
(t,{\bf w})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
and define

B^{t,{\bf w}}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace t)\negthinspace+\negthinspace B^{t}_{s\vee t}(\omega)
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega
.
There exists a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process
\mu^{o}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu^{o}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

on
\Omega_{0}
and an
{\cal N}_{\mu}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
such that for any
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\mu}
,

\mu_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{o}_{s}\big(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)
for a.e.
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)
.
It also holds for any
{\bf x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
and
\psi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{H}_{o}
that

P\big\{X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{s}(B^{t,{\bf w}}),\,\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1

and
E_{P}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{o}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
.

2
Stochastic Control/Stopping Problem with Expectation Constraints

Let
({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p})
be a probability space
equipped with a
d-
dimensional standard Brownian motion
\{{\cal B}_{s}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
.

Let
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
. We set

{\cal B}^{t}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal B}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal B}_{t}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
,
which is also a Brownian motion on
({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p})
.
Let
{\cal U}_{t}
collect all
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
progressively measurable processes
\mu\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

and let
{\cal S}_{t}
denote the set of all
[t,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
stopping times.
For any
({\bf x},\mu)\in\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal U}_{t}
,
Proposition
1.1
shows that the SDE with the open-loop control
\mu

\displaystyle{\cal X}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(t)+\int_{t}^{s}b\big(r,{\cal X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\sigma\big(r,{\cal X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)d{\cal B}_{r},~\,\forall\,s\in[t,\infty)\;\hbox{ with initial condition }{\cal X}\big|_{[0,t]}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}|_{[0,t]}

(2.1)

admits a unique strong solution
{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
on
\big({\cal Q},{\cal F},{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}},\mathfrak{p}\big)

(
i.e.,
{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}
is the unique
\big\{{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process solving SDE (
2.1
)
)
.

Let
f\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{H}_{o}
,
\{g_{i},h_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{H}_{o}

and let
\pi\negthinspace:[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace(-\infty,\infty]
be a Borel-measurable function bounded from below
by some
c_{\pi}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(-\infty,0)
.

Given a historical path
{\bf x}|_{[0,t]}
, the state of the game then evolves along process
\big\{{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

if the player chooses a control process
\mu\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal U}_{t}
.
The player also determines an exercise time
\tau\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal S}_{t}
to cease the game,
at which she will receive
an accumulative reward
\int_{t}^{\tau}\negthinspace f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\,dr
plus
a terminal reward
\pi\big(\tau,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\tau\land\cdot}\big)

(both random rewards can take negative values).
The player intends to maximize the expectation of her total wealth,
but her choice of
(\mu,\tau)
is subject to a series of expectation constraints

\displaystyle E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\quad E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},\quad\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}

(2.2)

for some
(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\{y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{z_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re
.
One can regard each
\int_{t}^{\tau}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr
or
\int_{t}^{\tau}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr
as certain accumulative cost.
So the value of this stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints (SCEC for short) is

\displaystyle V(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{(\mu,\tau)\in{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}\,E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau}f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\tau<\infty\}}\pi\big(\tau,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\tau\land\cdot}\big)\Big],

(2.3)

where
{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(\mu,\tau)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal U}_{t}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal S}_{t}\negthinspace:E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\,E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},\,\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}
.

To study the measurability of value function
V
and derive a dynamic programming principle for
V
without imposing any continuity condition on functions
f
,
\pi
,
g_{i}
’s and
h_{i}
’s in time and state variables, we follow
[
16
]
’s approach
to embed the controls, the stopping rules as well as the Brownian/state information into an enlarged canonical space
via a mapping
\omega\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\big({\cal B}_{\cdot}(\omega),\mu_{\cdot}(\omega),{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\cdot}(\omega),\tau(\omega)\big)

and consider their joint law as a new type of controls.

3
Weak Formulation

In this section, we study the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints in a weak formulation or over an enlarged canonical space

\displaystyle{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}.

As
\big(\mathbb{J},\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\big)
is a Borel space by Lemma
1.2
,

{\overline{\Omega}}
is also a Borel space under the product topology.
Let
\mathfrak{P}({\overline{\Omega}})
be the space of all probability measures on
\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})\big)

equipped with the topology of weak convergence, which is also a Borel space (see e.g. Corollary 7.25.1 of
[
6
]
).
For any
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}({\overline{\Omega}})
, set
\mathscr{B}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\Omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\big(\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}(\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}))\big)
.
We define the canonical coordinates on
{\overline{\Omega}}
by

\displaystyle\big({\overline{W}}_{\negthinspace s}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{U}}_{\negthinspace s}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}_{\negthinspace s}({\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\omega_{0}(s),\mathfrak{u}(s),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(s)\big),\quad s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\quad\hbox{and}\quad{\overline{T}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathfrak{t},\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{t}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}},

in which one can regard
{\overline{W}}
as a canonical coordinate for Brownian motion,

{\overline{U}}
as a canonical coordinate for the control process,

{\overline{X}}
as a canonical coordinate for the state process, and
{\overline{T}}
as a canonical coordinate for stopping rules.
Given
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, we define

\displaystyle{\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{\negthinspace s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{\negthinspace t}({\overline{\omega}})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big({\overline{U}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\big)dr\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,1),\quad\,\forall\,(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}.

The weak formulation of the SCEC relies on the following probability classes of
\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
.

Definition 3.1
.

For any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
let
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
be the collection of all probability measures
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
satisfying:

(
D1
)
There exists a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process

\widehat{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that

{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\mu}_{s}\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
,
where
\overline{\mu}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\overline{W}})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.

(
D2
)
The process
{\overline{W}}^{t}
is a
d-
dimensional Brownian motion on
\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}),{\overline{P}}\big)
.

(
D3
)

{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}_{s},~\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
,
where
\big\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

is an
\big\{{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process
that uniquely solves the following SDE with the open-loop control
\overline{\mu}
on
\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big),{\overline{P}}\big)\negthinspace:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{s}={\bf x}(t)+\int_{t}^{s}b\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\sigma\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}_{r}\big)d{\overline{W}}_{r},~\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\hbox{ with initial condition $\overline{\mathscr{X}}\big|_{[0,t]}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}\big|_{[0,t]}$. }

(3.1)

(
D4
)
There exists a
[t,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t},P_{0}}-
stopping time
\widehat{\tau}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that

{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\tau}({\overline{W}})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
.

Let
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
.
For any
s\in[t,\infty)
, define

{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{s}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace{\cal F}^{\overline{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s]\big)\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace\sigma\big({\overline{X}}_{r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,s]\big)
, which is countably generated by

\big\{{\overline{X}}^{-1}_{r}({\cal O})\negthinspace:r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace[0,t],{\cal O}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{l})\big\}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\big\{({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},{\overline{X}}_{r})^{{}^{-1}}({\cal O}^{\prime})\negthinspace:r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace(t,s],{\cal O}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\big\}
.
We denote the filtration
\big\{{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
by
{\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t}
.
Let
\widehat{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a

\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process on
\Omega_{0}
.
Then
\overline{\mu}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}\big({\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)
,
\,\forall\,(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
predictable process on
{\overline{\Omega}}
.
For any
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
,

\displaystyle\quad{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{\negthinspace s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s},{\overline{X}}_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{b}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace r},{\overline{X}}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace r},{\overline{X}}_{r})dr,\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

is an
{\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t}-
adapted continuous process and
{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s},{\overline{X}}_{s})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{a}\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)
is an
{\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t}-
stopping time.
We will simply denote
{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}({\bf 0})
by
{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}
.

Let us also define a shifted canonical process on
{\overline{\Omega}}
by
{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}_{\negthinspace\mathfrak{s}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{t}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}({\overline{\omega}})
,
\,\forall\,(\mathfrak{s},{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
.
(
Note: the subscript
\mathfrak{s}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
of
{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}
is the relative time after
t
while the subscript
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
of
{\overline{W}}^{t}
is the real time.
)

Given
\mathfrak{s}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty]
, one has
{\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{r}};\mathfrak{r}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\mathfrak{s}]\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\mathbb{R}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+\mathfrak{r}};\mathfrak{r}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\mathfrak{s}]\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\mathbb{R}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r};r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,t\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{s}]\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\mathbb{R}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}
.
In particular,
{\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}}_{\infty}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\infty}
. It then holds for any
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
that

{\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}({\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}}_{\infty})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\infty})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}
.

According to the martingale-problem formulation of controlled SDEs (Proposition
1.2
), we have an alternative description of
the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
:

Remark 3.1
.

Let
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
. In definition

of

(

)

)
is equivalent to

′
)

\mathbb{U}-

\ddot{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\ddot{\mu}_{\mathfrak{s}}\}_{\mathfrak{s}\in[0,\infty)}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that

{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\ddot{\mu}_{\mathfrak{s}}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\hbox{ for a.e. }\mathfrak{s}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
,
where
{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{U}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}
,
\,\forall\,\mathfrak{s}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
.

(

)

(

)
=

)

)
+

{\overline{P}}\{{\overline{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
and

\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

\,\forall\,(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}

.

Remark 3.2
.

Let
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}

and let
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}({\overline{\Omega}})
satisfy
(
D1
)
+
(

)
+
(

)
of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.

(
1
)
For any
\psi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{H}_{o}
, Proposition
1.3
shows that
E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}_{r}\big)dr\big]\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
.

(
2
)
Let
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}

\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded

the optional sampling theorem implies that
for any two

{\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t}-

{\overline{\zeta}}_{1},{\overline{\zeta}}_{2}

{\overline{\zeta}}_{1}\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{\zeta}}_{2}

{\overline{P}}-

\displaystyle E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big(\,{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{\overline{A}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\Big[E_{\overline{P}}\Big[{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\Big|{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{1}}\Big]{\bf 1}_{\overline{A}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0,~\;\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\zeta}}_{1}}.\quad

(3.2)

Let
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\{y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{z_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re

and set
{\overline{R}}(t)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{T}}\land t}^{\overline{T}}\negthinspace f(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}<\infty\}}\pi\big({\overline{T}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{T}}\land\cdot}\big)
.
Given a historical state path
{\bf x}|_{[0,t]}
, the value of the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints

\displaystyle E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\quad E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},\quad\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}

(3.3)

in weak formulation is

\displaystyle{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}\negthinspace f(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}<\infty\}}\pi\big({\overline{T}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{T}}\land\cdot}\big)\Big],

where
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace:E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\,E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},\,\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\Big\}
. We will simply call
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
the
weak
value of the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints.
In case
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\emptyset
,
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace-\infty
by the convention
\sup\emptyset\negthinspace:=\negthinspace-\infty
.

We can consider another weak value function of the SCEC:
Let
({\bf w},{\bf u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
and define

{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{W}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s),\,\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t];\,{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf u}(s)\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,t)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\big\}

as the subclass of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
given the historical Brownian path
{\bf w}\big|_{[0,t]}

and the historical control trajectory
{\bf u}\big|_{[0,t]}
.
The weak value of the stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints (
3.3
) given

({\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x})\big|_{[0,t]}
is

{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]
,
where
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{W}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s),\,\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t];\,{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf u}(s)\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,t)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\big\}
.

One of our main results in the next theorem demonstrates that
the value
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
in (
2.3
) coincides with the weak value
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
,
and is even equal to
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re
.
Then
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)={\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
, and

{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset\Leftrightarrow{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset\Leftrightarrow{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset
.

Theorem
3.1
indicates that the value of the SCEC is independent of a specific probabilistic setup
and is also indifferent to the Brownian/control history. This result even allows us to deal with the robust case:

Remark 3.3
.

Let
\big\{({\cal Q}_{\alpha},{\cal F}_{\alpha},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha})\big\}_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{A}}
be a family of probability spaces,
where
\mathfrak{A}
is a countable or uncountable index set
(
e.g. one can consider a non-dominated class
\{\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathfrak{A}}
of probability measures on a measurable space
({\cal Q},{\cal F})
)
.

Let
\alpha\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{A}
and let
{\cal B}^{\alpha}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal B}^{\alpha}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be a
d-
dimensional standard Brownian motion on
({\cal Q}_{\alpha},{\cal F}_{\alpha},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha})
. Given
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, set
{\cal B}^{\alpha,t}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal B}^{\alpha}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal B}^{\alpha}_{t}
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
,
let
{\cal U}^{\alpha}_{t}
collect all
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{\alpha,t},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}-
progressively measurable processes
\mu^{\alpha}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\mu^{\alpha}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

and let
{\cal S}^{\alpha}_{t}
denote the set of all
[t,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{\alpha,t},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}-
stopping times.
For any
({\bf x},\mu^{\alpha})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal U}^{\alpha}_{t}
,
let
{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be
the unique
\big\{{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{\alpha,t},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process
solving the SDE with the open-loop control
\mu^{\alpha}

\displaystyle{\cal X}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(t)+\int_{t}^{s}b(r,{\cal X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{\alpha}_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(r,{\cal X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{\alpha}_{r})d{\cal B}^{\alpha}_{r},~\,\forall\,s\in[t,\infty)\hbox{ with initial condition ${\cal X}\big|_{[0,t]}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}\big|_{[0,t]}$ }

on
\big({\cal Q}_{\alpha},{\cal F}_{\alpha},{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{\alpha,t},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}},\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}\big)
.

Then we know from Theorem
3.1
that for any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
and

(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\{y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{z_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re

\displaystyle\;\;{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{\alpha\in\mathfrak{A}}{\sup}\,\,\underset{(\mu^{\alpha},\tau_{\alpha})\in{\cal C}^{\alpha}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}\,E_{\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\alpha}}f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{\alpha}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\tau_{\alpha}<\infty\}}\pi\big(\tau_{\alpha},{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}_{\tau_{\alpha}\land\cdot}\big)\Big],

where
{\cal C}^{\alpha}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(\mu^{\alpha},\tau_{\alpha})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal U}^{\alpha}_{t}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal S}^{\alpha}_{t}\negthinspace:E_{\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\alpha}}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{\alpha}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\,E_{\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\alpha}}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{\alpha}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{\alpha}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},~\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}
.
To wit, the weak value
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
is also equal to the robust value of the SCEC
under model uncertainty.

The equivalence between strong and weak formulation of an (unconstrained) stochastic control/stopping problem was discussed in

[
18
]
. However, their argument may not be applicable to
stochastic control/stopping problem with expectation constraints:

Remark 3.4
.

When
\big(y_{i},h_{i}(\cdot,\cdot),z_{i}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace(\infty,0,0)
,
\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, the unconstrained version of Theorem
3.1
states that for any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,

V(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{(\mu,\tau)\in{\cal U}_{t}\times{\cal S}_{t}}{\sup}\,E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau}\negthinspace f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\tau<\infty\}}\pi\big(\tau,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\tau\land\cdot}\big)\big]
is equal to
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]
.
On the other hand,
[
18
]
showed that for any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,

V(t,{\bf x})
equals

{\overline{{\overline{V}}}}(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]
,
where
{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
collects all
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}({\overline{\Omega}})
satisfying
(
D1
)
,
(
D2
)
,
(
D3
)
and “
{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace t\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
”

(
We summarize
[
18
]
’s result in our terms for an easy comparison with our work
)
.
As
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
,
the equality
V(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]
indicates that the probability classes
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
’s are more accurate than
{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
’s to describe the
(
unconstrained
)
stochastic control/stopping problem in weak formulation.

The condition
(
D4
)
on
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
is necessary for the expectation-constraint case.
Without it, the weak value
{\overline{{\overline{V}}}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\\
\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]

(
with
{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace:E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}\negthinspace g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i},\,E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}\negthinspace h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i},\,\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\Big\}
)

may not be equal to
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
for the following reason:

In Proposition 4.3 of
[
18
]
, the key to show
{\overline{{\overline{V}}}}(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x})
or
E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x})
for a given
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
,
relies on transforming the hitting times of process
\big\{E_{\overline{P}}\big[{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}\in[t,s]\}}\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\big]\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

to a member of
{\cal S}_{t}
. More precisely,
the so-called Property
(
K
)
assures
an
{\bf F}^{W^{t},P_{0}}-
adapted càdlàg process
\widehat{\vartheta}_{\cdot}
such that

\widehat{\vartheta}_{s}({\overline{W}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}\in[t,s]\}}\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{s}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}\in[t,s]\}}\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\big]
,
{\overline{P}}-
a.s. for any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.
It follows that

E_{\overline{P}}\big[{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}\in[t,s]\}}\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}\in A\}}\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}_{\cdot}\in A\}}\widehat{\vartheta}_{s}({\overline{W}})\\
\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}{\bf 1}_{\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}\in A\}}\widehat{\vartheta}(dr,{\overline{W}})
,
{\overline{P}}-
a.s. for any
(s,A)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})
,
where
\overline{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{\overline{\mu}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\overline{W}})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is the
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
predictable process in
(
D1
)

and
\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
is the unique solution of SDE (
3.1
).
Let
\Phi
be a nonnegative Borel-measurable function on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
.
Then a standard approximation argument and the “change-of-variable” formula yield that

E_{\overline{P}}\big[\Phi({\overline{T}},{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}})\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\Phi(r,{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}})\widehat{\vartheta}(dr,{\overline{W}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{1}\Phi(\varrho({\overline{W}},\lambda),\widehat{\mu}({\overline{W}}),{\overline{X}})d\lambda
,
{\overline{P}}-
a.s.,
where
\varrho(\omega_{0},\lambda)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\widehat{\vartheta}_{s}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace>\negthinspace\lambda\big\}
,
\,\forall\,(\omega_{0},\lambda)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace(0,1)
.
Set
\mu_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\cal B})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.
Since the joint
{\overline{P}}-
distribution of
\big({\overline{W}},\widehat{\mu}_{\cdot}({\overline{W}})\big)
is equal to the joint
\mathfrak{p}-
distribution of
\big({\cal B},\widehat{\mu}_{\cdot}({\cal B})\big)
, we can deduce that
the joint
{\overline{P}}-
distribution of
\big({\overline{W}},\overline{\mu}_{\cdot},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}\big)
is equal to the joint
\mathfrak{p}-
distribution of
({\cal B},\mu_{\cdot},{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu})
and thus

\displaystyle E_{\overline{P}}\big[\Phi({\overline{T}},{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}})\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{1}E_{\overline{P}}\big[\Phi\big(\varrho({\overline{W}},\lambda),\widehat{\mu}({\overline{W}}),\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t,{\bf x},\overline{\mu}}\big)\big]d\lambda\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{1}E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\Phi(\varrho({\cal B},\lambda),\widehat{\mu}({\cal B}),{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu})\big]d\lambda.

(3.4)

As
\tau_{\lambda}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varrho({\cal B},\lambda)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal S}_{t}
for each
\lambda\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
, taking
\Phi
to be the total reward function
implies that

\displaystyle E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{1}E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\lambda}}\negthinspace f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\tau_{\lambda}<\infty\}}\pi\big(\tau_{\lambda},{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{\tau_{\lambda}\land\cdot}\big)\Big]d\lambda\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\int_{0}^{1}V(t,{\bf x})d\lambda\negthinspace=\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x}).

(3.5)

However, this argument is not applicable to the expectation-constraint case: Given a
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\overline{{\cal P}}}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
,
since
(\mu,\tau_{\lambda})
may not belong to
{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
for a.e.
\lambda\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
, one can not get
E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
like (
3.5
).
Actually, for each
\lambda\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
,
(\mu,\tau_{\lambda})
is only of
{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda})
with

(y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\{y^{i}_{\lambda}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{z^{i}_{\lambda}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)
and

(y^{i}_{\lambda},z^{i}_{\lambda})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\lambda}}\negthinspace g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big],E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\\
\int_{t}^{\tau_{\lambda}}\negthinspace h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\Big)
.
For
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, choosing accumulative cost functions for
\Phi
in (
3.4
)
renders that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\lambda}}\negthinspace g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\Big]d\lambda\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\bigg]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i}

and similarly

\int_{0}^{1}E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau_{\lambda}}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]d\lambda\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i}
, so
V\big(t,{\bf x},\{\int_{0}^{1}y_{\lambda}d\lambda\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{\int_{0}^{1}z_{\lambda}d\lambda\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
.
Then the attempt to show
E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{R}}(t)\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
reduces to deriving a Jensen-type inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}V(t,{\bf x},y_{\lambda},z_{\lambda})d\lambda\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V\Big(t,{\bf x},\Big\{\int_{0}^{1}y_{\lambda}d\lambda\Big\},\Big\{\int_{0}^{1}z_{\lambda}d\lambda\Big\}\Big).

But this does not hold since the value function
V
is not concave in level
z
of equality-type expectation constraints.

4
The Measurability of SCEC Values

In this section, using the martingale-problem formulation of controlled SDEs, we characterize the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
by countably many stochastic behaviors of the
canonical coordinators
({\overline{W}},{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}},{\overline{T}})
of
{\overline{\Omega}}
.
This will enable us to analyze the measurability of value functions of the stochastic control/stopping problem
with expectation constraints.

Let
\mathfrak{U}
be the equivalence classes of all
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
predictable processes
on
\Omega_{0}
in the sense that
\mu^{1},\mu^{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
are equivalent if
