\big\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big\}

is a
ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0}-
null set.
Given
\mu\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
, Fubini Theorem shows that

{\cal N}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\notin\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\big\}
is a
{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}-
measurable
set with zero
P_{0}-
measure or
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P_{0}}\big({\cal F}^{W}_{\infty}\big)
.
By modifying
\mu
on
{\cal N}
, we obtain an
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
predictable process with all paths in
\mathbb{J}
:

\{\mu_{s}{\bf 1}_{{\cal N}^{c}}\negthinspace+\negthinspace u_{0}{\bf 1}_{{\cal N}}\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
,
which is in the same equivalence class as
\mu
.
To wit, one can assume without loss of generality that for all
\mu\in\mathfrak{U}
,

\mu_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
for any
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
.
We equip
\mathfrak{U}
with the topology of local convergence in measure, i.e., the metric

\displaystyle\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{2}_{s})\big)ds\Big],\quad\,\forall\,\mu^{1},\,\mu^{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}\,.

Also, let
\mathfrak{S}
be the equivalence classes of all
[0,\infty]-
valued,

{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
stopping times on
\Omega_{0}

in the sense that
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}
are equivalent if

P_{0}\{\tau_{1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\tau_{2}\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
.
We endow
\mathfrak{S}
with the metric

\displaystyle\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\big[\rho_{\overset{}{+}}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\big],\qquad\,\forall\,\tau_{1},\,\tau_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}.

Lemma 4.1
.

\big(\mathfrak{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big)
and
\big(\mathfrak{S},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}\big)
are two complete separable metric spaces, i.e., Polish spaces.

For any
(\mu,\tau)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}
, define their joint distribution with
W
under
P_{0}
by

\Gamma(\mu,\tau)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace P_{0}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace(W,\mu,\tau)^{-1}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The mapping
\Gamma\negthinspace:\mathfrak{U}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)
is a continuous injection
from
\mathfrak{U}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}
into
\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)
.

For any
(t,{\overline{P}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
, define a probability measure on
\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T},\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T})\big)
by
{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}(D)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t},{\overline{T}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D\big\}
,
\,\forall\,D\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T})
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The mapping
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\ni(t,{\overline{P}})\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)
is continuous.

For
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
and
\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
, define process

\displaystyle{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{s},{\overline{X}}_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{b}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},{\overline{X}}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},{\overline{X}}_{r}\big)dr,\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty).

We can use Remark
3.1
and Lemma
4.2
to decompose the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
as the intersection of countably
many action sets of processes
({\overline{W}},{\overline{U}},{\overline{X}},{\overline{T}})
:

Proposition 4.1
.

For any
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
is the intersection of the following three subsets of
\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
:

i
)

{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{1}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\{{\overline{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\big\}
.

ii
)

{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{2}_{t}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Gamma(\mathfrak{U}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{S})\big\}
.

iii
)

{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{3}_{t}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\Big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\varphi)-{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\varphi)\Big)\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\prod}}{\bf 1}_{\{({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}},{\overline{X}}_{t+s_{i}})\in{\cal O}_{i}\}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0,~\,\forall\,(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N},\,\,\forall\,(\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r})\\
\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}^{2,<}_{+},\,\,\forall\,\{(s_{i},{\cal O}_{i})\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big(\mathbb{Q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace[0,\mathfrak{s}]\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\Big\}
.

Based on the countable decomposition of the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
by Proposition
4.1
,
the next proposition shows that the graph of probability classes
\{{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\}_{(t,{\bf x})\in[0,\infty)\times\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}}

is a Borel subset of
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
,
which is crucial for the measurability of the value functions
V\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{V}}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

The graph
\big\langle\negthinspace\big\langle{\overline{{\cal P}}}\big\rangle\negthinspace\big\rangle\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\big(t,{\bf x},{\overline{P}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\big\}
is a Borel subset of
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
.

Set
D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace:{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset\big\}

and
{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace:{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset\big\}
.

Corollary 4.1
.

The graph
{\big[\negthinspace\big[\,{\overline{{\cal P}}}\,\big]\negthinspace\big]}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\big(t,{\bf x},y,z,{\overline{P}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\big\}
is a Borel subset of
D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)

and the graph
{\big\{\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{{\cal P}}}\big\}\negthinspace\big\}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\big(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z,{\overline{P}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)\big\}
is a Borel subset of
{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
.

By Corollary
4.1
, the value function
{\overline{V}}
is upper semi-analytic and is thus universally measurable.

Theorem 4.1
.

The value function
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
is upper semi-analytic on
D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}

and the value function
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
is upper semi-analytic on
{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal P}}}
.

5
Dynamic Programming Principle for
{\overline{V}}

In this section, we explore a dynamic programming principles (DPP) for the value function
{\overline{V}}
in weak formulation,
which takes the conditional expected integrals of constraint functions as additional states.

Given
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, let
{\overline{\gamma}}
be a
[t,\infty)-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
stopping time and let
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
.
According to Lemma 1.3.3 and Theorem 1.1.8 of
[
39
]
,

{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}
is countably generated and there thus exists a family
\big\{{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\big\}_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}

of probability measures in
\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
, called the
regular conditional probability distribution
(r.c.p.d.) of
{\overline{P}}
with respect to
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}
, such that

(R1) for any
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})
, the mapping
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\big({\overline{A}}\big)
is
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}-
measurable;

(R2) for any
(-\infty,\infty]-
valued,
\mathscr{B}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\Omega}})-
measurable random variable
{\overline{\xi}}
that is bounded from below under
{\overline{P}}
,
it holds for any
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
except on a
{\overline{{\cal N}}}_{\overline{\xi}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\big)
that

{\overline{\xi}}
is
\mathscr{B}_{{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}({\overline{\Omega}})-
measurable and

E_{{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}\big[\,{\overline{\xi}}\,\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\,{\overline{\xi}}\,\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\big]({\overline{\omega}})
;

(R3) for some
{\overline{{\cal N}}}_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\big)
,

{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{A}}\}}
,
\,\forall\,\big({\overline{\omega}},{\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}
.

Let
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
and set
{{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:{\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}),~\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}})]\big\}
.
We know from Galmarino’s test that

\displaystyle{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}},

(5.1)

and
{{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}
is thus
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}-
measurable. Since
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}
for any
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
, (R3) shows that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\big({{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\big\{{\overline{\omega}}\in{{\overline{{\bf W}}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}}\big\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}_{0}.

(5.2)

For any
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, define

{\overline{Y}}^{i}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}({\overline{\gamma}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\int_{{\overline{T}}\land{\overline{\gamma}}}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\Big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\Big]
and

{\overline{Z}}^{i}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\gamma}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\int_{{\overline{T}}\land{\overline{\gamma}}}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\Big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\Big]
.
So
\big({\overline{Y}}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}({\overline{\gamma}}),{\overline{Z}}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(\big\{{\overline{Y}}^{i}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\big\{{\overline{Z}}^{i}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\Big)
is an
\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re-
valued
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}-
measurable random variable.

In terms of the r.c.p.d.
\big\{{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\big\}_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}
,
the probability class
\big\{{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace:(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}\big\}

is stable under conditioning as follows.
It will play an important role in deriving the sub-solution side of the DPP for
{\overline{V}}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Given
(t,{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
let
{\overline{\gamma}}
be a
[t,\infty)-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
stopping time and let
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.
There exists a
{\overline{P}}-
null set
{\overline{{\cal N}}}
such that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{\gamma}}\land\cdot}({\overline{\omega}})}\Big(\big({\overline{Y}}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big)({\overline{\omega}}),\big({\overline{Z}}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big)({\overline{\omega}})\Big),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}\big\}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}.

(5.3)

Now, we are ready to present a dynamic programming principle in weak formulation for
the value function
{\overline{V}}
, in which
\big({\overline{Y}}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}({\overline{\gamma}}),{\overline{Z}}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\gamma}})\big)
act as additional states for constraint levels at the intermediate horizon
{\overline{\gamma}}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Given
(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D_{\overline{{\cal P}}}
,
let
\big\{{{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\big\}_{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}
be a family of
[t,\infty)-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
stopping times. Then

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{{\overline{P}}\in{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)}{\sup}\,\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\bigg[{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}<{{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\}}\Big(\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}\negthinspace f(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\pi\big({\overline{T}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{T}}\land\cdot}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 65.44142pt+{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}\geq{{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\}}\bigg(\negthinspace\int_{t}^{{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\negthinspace f(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\overline{V}}\Big({{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}},{\overline{X}}_{{{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\land\cdot},{\overline{Y}}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}\big({{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\big),{\overline{Z}}_{\overline{P}}\big({{\overline{\gamma}}_{\overset{}{{\overline{P}}}}}\big)\Big)\bigg)\bigg].

6
Proofs

Proof of Lemma
1.1
:

Proposition 7.19 of
[
6
]
shows that
the topology of weak convergence
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
on
\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)

can be generated by a subbase
\Lambda\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{O_{\delta}(\mathfrak{m},\phi_{j})\negthinspace:\delta\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty),\mathfrak{m}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big),j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}
, where
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is a countable dense subset of
\widehat{C}_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
.
As the Borel space
\big(\mathfrak{P}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U})\big)\big)
is separable, it has a countable dense subset
\{\mathfrak{m}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

To show that
\widetilde{\Lambda}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\negthinspace:n,k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}

is another subbase of
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
, it suffices to verify that
any member of
\Lambda
is a union of some members in
\widetilde{\Lambda}
:
Let
(\delta,\mathfrak{m},j)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace O_{\delta}(\mathfrak{m},\phi_{j})
.
There exists
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that
\frac{2}{n}\negthinspace<\negthinspace\delta\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}(dt,du)\big)\big|
,
and one can find
\mathfrak{m}_{k}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime};\phi_{j})
for some
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
For any
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})
, we can deduce that

\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime\prime}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}_{k}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}_{k}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(dt,du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\frac{2}{n}\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}(dt,\\
du)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{m}(dt,du)\big)\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace\delta
,
which implies that
\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace O_{\delta}(\mathfrak{m},\phi_{j})
.

\Box

Proof of Lemma
1.2
: 1)
We first show that
\mathbb{J}
is a complete separable space under the metric

\displaystyle\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}(s),\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s))\big)ds,\quad\,\forall\,\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}.

1a)
Let
\{\mathfrak{u}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\big(\mathbb{J},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big)
.
We can find a subsequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
such that

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}},\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+1}}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2^{-k}
,
\,\forall\,k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
Fix
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. Since

\displaystyle 1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(a\negthinspace+\negthinspace b)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace 1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace a\negthinspace+\negthinspace 1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace b,\quad\,\forall\,a,b\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty),

(6.1)

it holds for any
\ell\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
that

1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+\ell}}(s))\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum^{\ell}_{i=1}1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i-1}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i}}(s))
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
,
taking
\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}
yields that

\displaystyle 1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+\ell}}(s)\big)\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\Big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+\ell}}(s)\big)\Big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i-1}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i}}(s)\big),\quad s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty).

(6.2)

The monotone convergence theorem then implies that

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\Big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+\ell}}(s))\Big)\Big)ds\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i-1}},\\
\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k+i}}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-k-i+1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 2^{-k+1}
.
So
\big\{n_{k}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
has a subsequence
\big\{n_{k_{m}}\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\,e^{-s}\Big(1\land\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s),\\
\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}+\ell}}(s))\Big)\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0

or
\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}+\ell}}(s)\big)\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0

for all
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
except on a
ds-
null set
{\cal N}
of
(0,\infty)
.
Given
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\backslash{\cal N}
, one has

\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\bigg(\underset{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{j+m}}}(s)\big)\bigg)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
,
i.e.,
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s)\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence
in
\mathbb{U}
. Let
\mathfrak{u}_{o}(s)
be the limit of
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s)\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\big(\mathbb{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big)
.

Define
\underline{\mu}(s)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s)\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,1]
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
which is a Borel measurable function on
[0,\infty)
. Then

\mathfrak{u}_{*}(s)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\underline{\mu}(s)\big){\bf 1}_{\{\underline{\mu}(s)\in\mathfrak{E}\}}\negthinspace+\negthinspace u_{0}{\bf 1}_{\{\underline{\mu}(s)\notin\mathfrak{E}\}}
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)

is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued Borel measurable function on
[0,\infty)
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{u}_{*}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
.
For
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\backslash{\cal N}
, the continuity of mapping
\mathscr{I}
shows

\underline{\mu}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{o}(s)\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{E}
, so
\mathfrak{u}_{*}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\underline{\mu}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{u}_{o}(s)
. The dominated convergence theorem implies
\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}},\mathfrak{u}_{*}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{s\in{\cal N}^{c}\}}e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}(s),\mathfrak{u}_{o}(s))\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.

Let
\varepsilon\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
. There exists a
N\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{n}},\mathfrak{u}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace\varepsilon/2
for any
\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace N
. We can also find a
m\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that
n_{k_{m}}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace N
and that

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}},\mathfrak{u}_{*}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace\varepsilon/2
.
It then holds for any
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
with
n\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace N
that

\displaystyle\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n},\mathfrak{u}_{*}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n},\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}}\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k_{m}}},\mathfrak{u}_{*}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace\varepsilon.

(6.3)

Hence,
\mathfrak{u}_{*}
is the limit of
\{\mathfrak{u}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\big(\mathbb{J},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big)
.

1b)
In this step, we demonstrate that
\mathbb{J}
is separable under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
.

Let
\{u_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable dense subset of
\big(\mathbb{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big)
and
let
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable base of the Euclidean topology on
[0,\infty)
.
Given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let us enumerate the
2^{n}
elements of
\big\{\underset{i\in I}{\cup}O_{i}\negthinspace:I\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\{1,\cdots,n\}\big\}
by
\big\{\breve{O}^{n}_{1},\cdots,\breve{O}^{n}_{2^{n}}\big\}
and consider
the following countable collections of
\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)/\mathscr{B}[-1,1]-
measurable functions on
[0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{\mathscr{I}(u_{j}){\bf 1}_{\breve{O}^{n}_{i}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{(\breve{O}^{n}_{i})^{c}}\negthinspace:i\in\{1,\cdots,2^{n}\},~j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\Big\},\quad\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{\underset{i=1,\cdots,k}{\max}\mathfrak{x}_{i}\negthinspace:k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N},~\{\mathfrak{x}_{1},\cdots,\mathfrak{x}_{k}\}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{C}_{n}\Big\}.

Clearly, each
\mathfrak{x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}
takes values in a finite subset of
\big\{\mathscr{I}(u_{i})\big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\{-1\}
.
By additionally assigning
\mathscr{I}^{-1}(-1)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace u_{0}
, one has

\widetilde{{\bf C}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\mathscr{I}^{-1}(\mathfrak{x})\negthinspace:\mathfrak{x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\hbox{ for some }n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big\{\hbox{$\{u_{i}\}^{\infty}_{i=0}-$valued $\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)-$measurable functions}\big\}\negthinspace=:\negthinspace\widehat{{\bf C}}
.

We claim that
\widehat{{\bf C}}
is a dense subset of
\mathbb{J}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
.
To see this, for any
i,n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
we set
o^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{u\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{U}:\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(u,u_{i})\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2^{-n}\big\}

as the open ball centered at
u_{i}
with radius
2^{-n}
.
We also set
\widetilde{o}^{n}_{1}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace o^{n}_{1}
and
\widetilde{o}^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace o^{n}_{i}\big\backslash\Big(\underset{j<i}{\cup}o^{n}_{j}\Big)
for
i\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace 2
.
Given
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
and
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, define a member of
\widehat{{\bf C}}

by
\mathfrak{u}_{n}(s)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{s\in{\cal E}^{n}_{i}\}}u_{i}
,

s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, where

{\cal E}^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace:\mathfrak{u}(s)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{o}^{n}_{i}\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)
.
As
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n}(s),\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{s\in{\cal E}^{n}_{i}\}}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(u_{i},\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2^{-n}
for any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
one has
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n},\mathfrak{u}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n}(s),\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace 2^{-n}
. So
\widehat{{\bf C}}
is a dense subset of
\mathbb{J}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
.

We then show that the countable collection
\widetilde{{\bf C}}
is dense in
\widehat{{\bf C}}
and is thus dense in
\mathbb{J}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
:
Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widehat{{\bf C}}
and
\varepsilon\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
.
We set
\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace:\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{i}\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)
,
\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
Since
\lambda({\cal E})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{s\in{\cal E}}\negthinspace e^{-s}ds

is a probability measure on
\big([0,\infty),\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\big)
, there exists
N\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that

\lambda\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)<\varepsilon/3
.

Given
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots\negthinspace,N
, Proposition 7.17 of
[
6
]
shows that

\lambda({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i})\negthinspace<\negthinspace\frac{\varepsilon}{3N}
for some open subset
{\cal O}_{i}
of
[0,\infty)
containing
\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}
.
Since
{\cal O}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}O_{\ell^{\,i}_{n}}
for some subsequence
\big\{\ell^{\,i}_{n}\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

of
\mathbb{N}
, one can find
n_{i}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that

\lambda\big({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)<\frac{\varepsilon}{3N}
,
where
\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{n=1}{\overset{n_{i}}{\cup}}O_{\ell^{\,i}_{n}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)
.
As
\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\big\{\breve{O}^{\mathfrak{n}}_{j}\big\}^{2^{\mathfrak{n}}}_{j=1}
for
\mathfrak{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{i=1,\cdots,N}{\max}\ell^{\,i}_{n_{i}}
,
we see that
\mathfrak{x}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}(u_{i}){\bf 1}_{\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\breve{{\cal O}}^{c}_{i}}
belongs to
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
Define
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\Big(\underset{i=1,\cdots,N}{\max}\,\mathfrak{x}_{i}\Big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{{\bf C}}
.

For
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots\negthinspace,N
, if
{\cal A}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)\big\backslash\Big(\underset{j\leq N;j\neq i}{\cup}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)
is not empty, it holds for any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}_{i}
that

\mathfrak{x}_{j}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{j=i\}}\mathscr{I}(u_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{j\neq i\}}
for
j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{1,\cdots\negthinspace,N\}
and thus

\mathfrak{u}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{x}_{i}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(s)
.
Also, if
{\cal A}_{0}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)^{c}
is not empty, it holds for any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}_{0}

that
\mathfrak{u}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}(-1)\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(s)
. Then

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u},\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{s\in{\cal A}}e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}(s),\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}(s)\big)\big)ds

for
{\cal A}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=0}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}(\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\backslash{\cal A}_{i})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\bigg(\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)\bigg)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,\infty)
.
Since

\displaystyle\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\backslash{\cal A}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}^{c}_{i}\big)\hbox{$\bigcup$}\Big(\underset{j\leq N;j\neq i}{\cup}\big(\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\big)\Big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)\hbox{$\bigcup$}\Big(\underset{j\leq N;j\neq i}{\cup}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\backslash\widehat{{\cal E}}_{j}\Big),\quad i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,N

and since
\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\Big(\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)^{c}\Big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\big(\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\widehat{{\cal E}}^{c}_{j}\big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\big({\cal O}_{j}\backslash\widehat{{\cal E}}_{j}\big)
,
we can deduce that

{\cal A}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal O}_{i}\backslash\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{{\cal E}}_{i}\Big)
.
It follows that
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u},\widehat{\mathfrak{u}}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\lambda({\cal A})\negthinspace<\negthinspace\varepsilon
.
Namely, the countable collection
\widetilde{{\bf C}}
is dense in
\widehat{{\bf C}}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
.

Therefore,
\big(\mathbb{J},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big)
is a complete separable metric space.

2)
We next show that the mapping
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\negthinspace:\big(\mathbb{J},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big)\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\Big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)\Big)
is a continuous injection.

2a)
Let
\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
such that
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})
. Since
\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{U}}{\bf 1}_{\{s\leq t\}}\mathscr{I}(u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u})(ds,du)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{U}}{\bf 1}_{\{s\leq t\}}\mathscr{I}(u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})\\
(ds,du)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s)\big)ds
for any
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
,
we see that
e^{-s}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace e^{-s}\mathscr{I}\big(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s)\big)
and thus

\mathfrak{u}(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s)
for a.e.
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
, namely,
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{J}
. So
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
is an injection.

2b)
Let
\{\mathfrak{u}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{J}
converging to a
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}

and we show that
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n})
converges to
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u})
under
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
.
By e.g. Lemma 7.6 of
[
6
]
,
this is equivalent to verify that

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi(s,u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n})(ds,du)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi(s,u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u})(ds,du)

(6.4)

for any bounded continuous function
\phi\negthinspace:[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
.

Let
\phi
be such a continuous function on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
For the limit (
6.4
), it suffices to show that any subsequence
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of

\{\mathfrak{u}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
has in turn a subsequence
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n^{\prime}_{k}}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying (
6.4
):
As
\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
,
there exists a subsequence
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n^{\prime}_{k}}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\big\{\mathfrak{u}_{n_{k}}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n^{\prime}_{k}}(s),\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
for a.e.

s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
.
Then the continuity of
\phi
and the dominated convergence theorem imply that

\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi(s,u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mathfrak{u}_{n^{\prime}_{k}}\big)(ds,du)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\phi\big(s,\mathfrak{u}_{n^{\prime}_{k}}(s)\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\phi\big(s,\mathfrak{u}(s)\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi(s,u)\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u})(ds,du)
.

So
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
is a continuous injection from the complete separable metric space
\big(\mathbb{J},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big)
to the Borel space
\Big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)\Big)
.
(In particular, the topology induced by
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
is stronger than
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
.)
Then the image
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathbb{J})
is a Lusin subset and thus a Borel subset
of
\Big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)\Big)
,
see e.g. Theorem A.6 of
[
40
]
.

As the embedding mapping
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}
is clearly a homeomorphism between

\big(\mathbb{J},\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\big)
and
\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathbb{J})
with the relative topology to
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)
,
we obtain that
\big(\mathbb{J},\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\big)
is a Borel space.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
1.3
: 1)

For any
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
and
\phi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
,
set
{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{t}\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds
,
\,\forall\,\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
.
We first show that
\mathfrak{F}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal I}^{\phi}_{t};t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty),\phi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C_{b}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
.

1a)
Let
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
,
\phi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
and
a\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
.
To see that
A\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\{\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace:{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace<\negthinspace a\}
belongs to
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
,
we let
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A
and set
\varepsilon\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\big(a\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\big)
.
There exists a positive bounded continuous function
\beta
on
[0,\infty)
such that

\beta(s)\negthinspace=\negthinspace e^{s}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]
and
\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-s}\beta(s)ds\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\big(\|\phi\|_{\infty}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace 1\big)^{-1}
,
where
\|\phi\|_{\infty}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{(s,u)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{U}}{\sup}\,|\phi(s,u)|
.
Set
{\cal O}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\varepsilon}(\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}(\mathfrak{u}),\beta\phi)\big)
, which clearly contains
\mathfrak{u}
.
Let
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal O}
. We can deduce that

\displaystyle\varepsilon\negthinspace>\negthinspace\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\beta(s)\big(\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s))\negthinspace-\negthinspace\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\Big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\big(\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s))\negthinspace-\negthinspace\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\Big|\negthinspace-\negthinspace\Big|\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-s}\beta(s)\big(\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s))\negthinspace-\negthinspace\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\Big|.

Since

\big|\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\beta(s)\big(\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}(s))\negthinspace-\negthinspace\phi(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))\big)ds\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace 2\|\phi\|_{\infty}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-s}\beta(s)ds\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\varepsilon
,
one has
\big|{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2\varepsilon
, which implies that
{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u}^{\prime})\negthinspace<\negthinspace a
or
\mathfrak{u}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A
.
So
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal O}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace A
. As the induced topology
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
is generated by the subbase

\Big\{\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\delta}(\mathfrak{m},\phi)\big)
,

\,\forall\,(\delta,\mathfrak{m},\phi)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\Big\}
,
we see that
A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
.
It follows that
\{\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace:{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal E}\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
for any
{\cal E}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})

and thus
\mathfrak{F}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal I}^{\phi}_{t};t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty),\phi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C_{b}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U})\big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
.

1b)
Let
\big\{\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\big\}_{n,k,j\in\mathbb{N}}

be the countable subbase of
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
in (
1.1
).
Given
n,k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, let
\{I^{j}_{t}\}_{t\in(0,\infty)}
denote
\{{\cal I}^{\phi}_{t}\}_{t\in(0,\infty)}
with
\phi(s,u)\negthinspace=\negthinspace e^{-s}\phi_{j}(s,u)
,

\,\forall\,(s,u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
and set
\kappa_{k,j}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{U}}\phi_{j}(t,u)\mathfrak{m}_{k}(dt,du)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
.
Since the function
I^{j}_{*}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{t\to\infty}{\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}}I^{j}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})
,
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
\mathfrak{F}-
measurable and
since
\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\phi_{j}(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{t\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-s}\phi_{j}(s,\mathfrak{u}(s))ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{t\to\infty}{\lim}I^{j}_{t}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace=\negthinspace I^{j}_{*}(\mathfrak{u})
holds for any
\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
,
(
1.1
) shows that

\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace:\big|I^{j}_{*}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\kappa_{k,j}\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace 1/n\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}
.
Then
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}
and thus
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}
.

2)
We demonstrate the second statement in several steps. Then
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big(I_{\varphi};\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)\big)
easily follows.

Let
\{\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable dense subset of
\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}

and let
\big\{\,\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j}\negthinspace=\negthinspace(w_{j},x_{j})\big\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable dense subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
.
Given
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, we set
O^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace:\rho_{\overset{}{\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}}}(\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{i})\negthinspace<\negthinspace 1/n\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})

and
\widetilde{O}^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace O^{n}_{i}\big\backslash\Big(\underset{i^{\prime}<i}{\overset{}{\cup}}O^{n}_{i^{\prime}}\Big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})
for any
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
We also denote
{\cal E}^{n}_{j}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j})\Big\backslash\Big(\underset{j^{\prime}<j}{\overset{}{\cup}}O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j^{\prime}})\Big)

for any
j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.

2a)
Let
\phi\negthinspace:[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace[0,c_{\phi}]
be a continuous function
for some
c_{\phi}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(1,\infty)
.

Let
T\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
and
m\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. We set
t^{m}_{k}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace k2^{-m}T
,
\,\forall\,k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{0,1,\cdots,2^{m}\}
.
Then, we let
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{0,1,\cdots,2^{m}\negthinspace-\negthinspace 1\}
.

For any
i,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, as the function
\phi_{i,j}(s,u)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\phi(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{i},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j},u)
,
(s,u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
is of
C_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
,
we know from Part (1) that

I^{m,k}_{i,j}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal I}^{\phi_{ij}}_{t^{m}_{k+1}}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal I}^{\phi_{ij}}_{t^{m}_{k}}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t^{m}_{k}}^{t^{m}_{k+1}}\phi\big(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{i},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j},\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr
,
\,\forall\,\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Since the function
\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t^{m}_{k},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})
is continuous in
\omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}

and since the function
{\cal W}^{m}_{k}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\omega_{0}(t^{m}_{k}),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(t^{m}_{k})\big)
is continuous in
(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
we can deduce from the continuity of
\phi
and the bounded convergence theorem that the mapping

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\Phi^{m}_{k}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t^{m}_{k}}^{t^{m}_{k+1}}\negthinspace\phi\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t^{m}_{k},\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(t^{m}_{k}),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(t^{m}_{k}),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\negthinspace\int_{t^{m}_{k}}^{t^{m}_{k+1}}\negthinspace\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t^{m}_{k},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\in\widetilde{O}^{n}_{i}\}}{\bf 1}_{\{{\cal W}^{m}_{k}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\in{\cal E}^{n}_{j}\}}\phi\big(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}^{i},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j},\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t^{m}_{k},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\in\widetilde{O}^{n}_{i}\}}{\bf 1}_{\{{\cal W}^{m}_{k}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\in{\cal E}^{n}_{j}\}}I^{m,k}_{i,j}(\mathfrak{u}),\quad\,\forall\,(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\quad

is
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Then the continuity of
\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}})
in
r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
and the bounded convergence theorem imply that the mapping

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\Phi(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{0}^{T}\phi\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{T}\sum^{2^{m}-1}_{k=0}{\bf 1}_{\{r\in[t^{m}_{k},t^{m}_{k+1})\}}\phi\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(t^{m}_{k},\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(t^{m}_{k}),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(t^{m}_{k}),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\sum^{2^{m}-1}_{k=0}\Phi^{m}_{k}(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u}),\quad\,\forall\,(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}

is also
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.

2b)
Set
\mathbb{X}_{1}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
\mathbb{X}_{2}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
,
\mathbb{X}_{3}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
and
\mathbb{X}_{4}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
. They are all metric spaces.
Let
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,2,3,4
. We denote the corresponding metric of
\mathbb{X}_{i}
by
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{X}_{i}}}
and Let
C_{i}
be a closed subset of
\mathbb{X}_{i}

Given
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, we define an open subset
O^{i}_{n}
of
\mathbb{X}_{i}
by
O^{i}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{X}_{i}\negthinspace:dist_{\overset{}{\mathbb{X}_{i}}}(x,C_{i})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{x^{\prime}\in C_{i}}{\inf}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{X}_{i}}}(x,x^{\prime})\negthinspace<\negthinspace 1/n\Big\}
. In light of Urysohn’s Lemma,
there exist continuous function
\phi^{i}_{n}\negthinspace:\mathbb{X}_{i}\to[0,1]
such that

\phi^{i}_{n}(x)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
,
\,\forall\,x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C_{i}
and
\phi^{i}_{n}(x)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
,
\,\forall\,x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(O^{i}_{n})^{c}
.
So
\widehat{\phi}_{n}(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\phi^{1}_{n}(s)\phi^{2}_{n}(\omega_{\overset{}{X}})\phi^{3}_{n}(\,\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,)\phi^{4}_{n}(u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,1]
,
(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}

is a continuous function satisfying
\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\widehat{\phi}_{n}(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\,\rule[-1.13809pt]{0.4pt}{4.83694pt}\hskip-0.35pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{3.41432pt}{0.4pt}\hskip-2.41849pt\rule[1.05273pt]{1.70717pt}{0.4pt}\hskip-2.41849pt\rule[3.27205pt]{3.41432pt}{0.4pt}\hskip-0.35pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{4.83694pt}\,,u)\in C_{1}\times C_{2}\times C_{3}\times C_{4}\}}
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.

Let
T\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
. Since the mappings
\int_{0}^{T}\widehat{\phi}_{n}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr
,

(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
are
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable for all
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
by Part (2a),
the bounded convergence theorem shows that the function

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}{\bf 1}_{\{(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r))\in C_{1}\times C_{2}\times C_{3}\times C_{4}\}}dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{T}\widehat{\phi}_{n}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr,

(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is also
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Let
{\cal H}
collect all real-valued Borel-measurable functions
\psi
on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}

such that the mapping
\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\ni(\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\int_{0}^{T}\psi\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr
is
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Clearly,
{\cal H}
is closed under linear combination.
If
\{\psi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\cal H}
is a sequence of non-negative functions that increases to a bounded function
\psi

on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
,
the bounded convergence theorem implies that
\psi
is also of
{\cal H}
.
Since
\{C_{1}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace C_{2}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace C_{3}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace C_{4}\negthinspace:\hbox{$C_{1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,\infty)$, $C_{2}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}$, $C_{3}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}$ and $C_{4}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathbb{U}$ are closed}\}

is a Pi-system that generates
\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{U})
, we know from the monotone class theorem that

{\cal H}
includes all bounded Borel-measurable functions on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.

2c)
Let
\psi\negthinspace:(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace[-\infty,\infty]
be a Borel-measurable function
and set
s^{m}_{k}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace k2^{-m}
for any
m\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\{0\}
.
Given
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, the bounded convergence theorem renders that
the mapping
\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}n\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\psi^{\pm}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\\
\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\sum^{\infty}_{k,\ell=0}{\bf 1}_{\{t\in[s^{m}_{k},s^{m}_{k+1})\}}{\bf 1}_{\{\mathfrak{s}\in[s^{m}_{\ell},s^{m}_{\ell+1})\}}\int_{s^{m}_{k}}^{s^{m}_{k+\ell}}n\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\psi^{\pm}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr
,
(t,\mathfrak{s},\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\\
\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,\infty)\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{\overset{}{X}})\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Then it follows from the monotone convergence theorem that
the mapping
\Psi^{\pm}(t,\mathfrak{s},\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}\psi^{\pm}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{t}^{t+\mathfrak{s}}n\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\psi^{\pm}\big(r,\mathfrak{l}_{2}(r,\omega_{\overset{}{X}}),\omega_{0}(r),\omega_{\overset{}{X}}(r),\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr

is Borel-measurable in
(t,\mathfrak{s},\omega_{0},\omega_{\overset{}{X}},\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
,
proving the second statement.

Clearly,
\mathfrak{F}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\sigma\big(I_{\varphi};\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big(I_{\varphi};\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)\big)
,
where
L^{0}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)
collect all real
-
valued Borel-measurable functions on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
Let
\varphi
be a non-negative function in
L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)
.
Taking
\psi(r,\mathfrak{x},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi(r,u)
,
\,\forall\,(r,\mathfrak{x},\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,,u)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
shows that the mapping
I_{\varphi}(\mathfrak{u})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{T\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{T}\varphi\big(r,\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{T\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{0}^{T}\psi\big(r,{\bf 0},0,\mathfrak{u}(r)\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{T\to\infty}{\lim}\Psi(0,T,{\bf 0},{\bf 0},\mathfrak{u})
,
\,\forall\,\mathfrak{u}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable.
Hence, we have
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{F}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\big(I_{\varphi};\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)\big)
.

\Box

Proof of Proposition
1.2
: 1)
Set
{\cal N}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega\negthinspace:X_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace{\bf x}(s)\hbox{ for some }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{X}_{t})
and let
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
.
We set
c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{|(w,x)|\leq n+\mathfrak{a}}{\sup}\big(\sum^{2}_{i=0}|D^{i}\varphi(w,x)|\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\varphi(0,{\bf x}(t))\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
and
c^{n}_{t,{\bf x}}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big[d/2\negthinspace+\negthinspace\kappa(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)(\|{\bf x}\|_{t}\negthinspace+\negthinspace n\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\kappa^{2}(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)(\|{\bf x}\|_{t}\negthinspace+\negthinspace n\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{a})^{2}\big]n\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+n}\underset{u\in\mathbb{U}}{\sup}\,\big(|b(r,{\bf 0},u)|\negthinspace+\negthinspace|\sigma(r,{\bf 0},u)|^{2}\big)dr\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
.
Given
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
, since
\big\|X_{r\land\cdot}(\omega)\big\|_{r}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{r^{\prime}\in[0,r]}{\sup}\,\big|X_{r^{\prime}}(\omega)\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\|{\bf x}\|_{t}\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace(n\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{a})
,
\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\big[t,(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)\big]
,
we can deduce from (
1.2
), (
1.3
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\underset{s\in[t,(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)]}{\sup}\,\big|\big(M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\varphi)\big)(\omega)\big|

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\underset{s\in[t,(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)]}{\sup}\,\big|\varphi\big(B^{t}_{s}(\omega),X_{s}(\omega)\big)\big|\negthinspace+\negthinspace c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\int_{t}^{(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)}\negthinspace\Big(\big|b(r,X_{r\land\cdot}(\omega),\mu_{r}(\omega))\big|\negthinspace+\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\big(d\negthinspace+\negthinspace\big|\sigma(r,X_{r\land\cdot}(\omega),\mu_{r}(\omega))\big|^{2}\big)\Big)dr

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace+\negthinspace c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\int_{t}^{(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)}\Big(\kappa(r)\big\|X_{r\land\cdot}(\omega)\big\|_{r}\negthinspace+\negthinspace|b(r,{\bf 0},\mu_{r}(\omega))|\negthinspace+\negthinspace d/2\negthinspace+\negthinspace\kappa^{2}(r)\big\|X_{r\land\cdot}(\omega)\big\|^{2}_{r}\negthinspace+\negthinspace|\sigma(r,{\bf 0},\mu_{r}(\omega))|^{2}\Big)dr

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\big(1\negthinspace+\negthinspace c^{n}_{t,{\bf x}}(\mathfrak{a})\big).\quad

(6.5)

So the process
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is bounded.

2)
We next show that (i) implies (ii): Suppose that (i) holds and let
(\varphi,n,\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
. Setting
\theta\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(t,{\bf x},\mu)
,
we simply denote
\Xi^{\theta}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(B^{t}_{s},X^{\theta}_{s})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

and set
\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:|\Xi^{\theta}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathfrak{a}|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)
,
which is an
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
stopping time.
Applying Itô’s formula yields that
P-
a.s.

\displaystyle M^{\theta}_{s}(\varphi)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\varphi(\Xi^{\theta}_{s})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{b}\big(r,X^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi(\Xi^{\theta}_{r})dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,X^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi(\Xi^{\theta}_{r})dr

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\varphi\big(0,{\bf x}(t)\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}D\varphi(\Xi^{\theta}_{r})\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\big(r,X^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dB_{r},\quad s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty).

For any
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega
, an analogy to (
6.5
) shows that

\underset{s\in[t,(\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)]}{\sup}\,\big|\big(M^{\theta}_{s}(\varphi)\big)(\omega)\big|\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})\big(1\negthinspace+\negthinspace c^{n}_{t,{\bf x}}(\mathfrak{a})\big)
and
\int_{t}^{(\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)}\big|D\varphi(\Xi^{\theta}_{r}(\omega))\cdot\overline{\sigma}(r,X^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot}(\omega),\mu_{r}(\omega))\big|^{2}dr\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace(c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))^{2}\big[d\negthinspace+\negthinspace 2\kappa^{2}(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)(\|{\bf x}\|_{t}\negthinspace+\negthinspace n\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{a})^{2}\big]n\negthinspace+\negthinspace 2(c^{\varphi}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))^{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{t+n}\underset{u\in\mathbb{U}}{\sup}\,|\sigma(r,{\bf 0},u)|^{2}dr\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
.
So

\big\{M^{\theta}_{s\land\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
martingale.

(6.6)

Set
{\cal N}_{\theta}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega\negthinspace:X_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace X^{\theta}_{s}(\omega)\hbox{ for some }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{t,P}_{\infty})
.
For any
(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\theta}
,

\displaystyle X^{\theta}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace X_{s}(\omega),\quad\big(M^{\theta}_{s\vee t}(\varphi)\big)(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(M^{t,\mu}_{s\vee t}(\varphi)\big)(\omega)\quad\hbox{and thus}\quad(\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace(\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a}))(\omega).

(6.7)

Fix
t_{1},t_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
with
t_{1}\negthinspace<\negthinspace t_{2}
.
Let
\big\{(s_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\big\}^{m}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[t,t_{1}]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\big\{(r_{j},A_{j})\big\}^{k}_{j=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,t_{1}]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{l})
.
We can derive from (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) that

E_{P}\Big[{\bf 1}_{{\cal N}^{c}_{\theta}}\big(M^{t,\mu}_{t_{2}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{t_{1}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big)\prod^{m}_{i=1}{\bf 1}_{(B^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})}\prod^{k}_{j=1}{\bf 1}_{X_{r_{j}}^{-1}(A_{j})}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{P}\Big[{\bf 1}_{{\cal N}^{c}_{\theta}}\big(M^{\theta}_{t_{2}\land\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\\
\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{\theta}_{t_{1}\land\tau^{\theta}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big)\prod^{m}_{i=1}{\bf 1}_{(B^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})}\prod^{k}_{j=1}{\bf 1}_{(X^{\theta}_{r_{j}})^{-1}(A_{j})}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
So the Lambda-system
\Lambda\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{t,P}_{\infty}\negthinspace:E_{P}\big[\big(M^{t,\mu}_{t_{2}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{t_{1}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{A}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\big\}
contains the Pi-system

\Big\{\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{m}{\cap}}(B^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})\Big)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}X_{r_{j}}^{-1}(A_{j})\Big)\negthinspace:\big\{(s_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\big\}^{m}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[t,t_{1}]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,\big\{(r_{j},A_{j})\big\}^{k}_{j=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,t_{1}]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{l})\Big\}
,
which generates
{\cal F}^{t}_{t_{1}}
. Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem (see e.g Theorem 3.2 of
[
7
]
) renders
{\cal F}^{t}_{t_{1}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Lambda
.
As
{\cal F}^{t}_{t_{1}}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}\big({\cal F}^{t}_{\infty}\big)
is another Pi-system included in
\Lambda
. Applying Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem again yields that
{\cal F}^{t,P}_{t_{1}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\sigma\big({\cal F}^{t}_{t_{1}}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{t}_{\infty})\big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Lambda
, i.e.,

E_{P}\big[\big(M^{t,\mu}_{t_{2}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{t_{1}\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{A}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
,
\,\forall\,A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{t,P}_{t_{1}}
.
Hence,
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}(\mathfrak{a})}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingale,
which is further a
{\bf F}^{t}-
martingale if process
\mu
is
