{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-
progressively measurable.

3)
As
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
, (ii)
\mathop{\Rightarrow}
(iii) is straightforward. It remains to show that (iii) gives rise to (i).

If the process
\mu
is
{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-
progressively measurable and if
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{t}-
martingale for any
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, then Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem implies that

\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is further an
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingale for any
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.

3a)
Define
{\cal F}^{t,P}_{s+}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{\varepsilon>0}{\overset{}{\cap}}{\cal F}^{t,P}_{s+\varepsilon}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

and set
{\bf G}^{t,P}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\cal G}^{t,P}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{t,P}_{s+}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
.

Let
i,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{1,\cdots\negthinspace,d\}
. We set
\phi_{i}(w,x)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace w_{i}
and
\phi_{ij}(w,x)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace w_{i}w_{j}

for any
w\negthinspace=\negthinspace(w_{1},\cdots\negthinspace,w_{d})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Clearly,
\phi_{i},\phi_{ij}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
.
One can calculate that
M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t,i}_{s}
,
M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t,i}_{s}B^{t,j}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\delta_{ij}(s\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
,
where
B^{t}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(B^{t,1}_{s},\cdots\negthinspace,B^{t,d}_{s}\big)
and
\delta_{ij}
is the
(i,j)-
element of the identity matrix
I_{d\times d}
.

Let
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. By (iii),
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{i})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
and
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s\land\tau^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{ij})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
are bounded
{\bf F}^{t,P}-
martingales. The
optional sampling
theorem (e.g. Theorem 1.3.22 of
[
21
]
) implies that
they are further
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
martingales.
Since
\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\negthinspace\mathop{\uparrow}\,\tau^{t}_{n}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\infty
,
we see that
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t,i}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

and
\big\{M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t,i}_{s}B^{t,j}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\delta_{ij}(s\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
are
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
local martingales.
Lévy’s characterization theorem then yields that
B^{t}
is a Brownian motion with respect to filtration
{\bf G}^{t,P}

and is thus a Brownian motion with respect to filtration
{\bf F}^{B^{t}}
.

3b)

We simply denote
\Xi_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(B^{t}_{s},X_{s})
,
\beta_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\overline{b}\big(s,X_{s\land\cdot},\mu_{s}\big)
and

\alpha_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(s,X_{s\land\cdot},\mu_{s}\big)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.
Let
i,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{1,\cdots\negthinspace,d\negthinspace+\negthinspace l\}
. We set
\psi_{i}(\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{i}

and
\psi_{ij}(\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{i}\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{j}

for any
\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{1},\cdots\negthinspace,\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.45pt}{7.39772pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[2.27621pt]{2.84526pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-3.98337pt\rule[5.69054pt]{5.12149pt}{0.45pt}\hskip-0.4pt\rule[-1.42262pt]{0.4pt}{7.39772pt}\,_{d+l}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d+l}
.
Similar to
M^{t,\mu}_{\cdot}(\phi_{i})
and
M^{t,\mu}_{\cdot}(\phi_{ij})
,
the processes
M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{i})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Xi^{(i)}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(i)}_{r}dr
and

M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{ij})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Xi^{(i)}_{s}\Xi^{(j)}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(i)}_{r}\Xi^{(j)}_{r}dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(j)}_{r}\Xi^{(i)}_{r}dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}(\alpha_{r})_{ij}dr
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

are
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
local martingales.
Using the
integration by parts
formula, we obtain that
P-
a.s.

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\Xi^{(i)}_{s}\Xi^{(j)}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{i})M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{j})\negthinspace=\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{i})\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(j)}_{r}dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{j})\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(i)}_{r}dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(i)}_{r}dr\cdot\int_{t}^{s}\beta^{(j)}_{r}dr

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}M^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{i})\beta^{(j)}_{r}dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(j)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{i})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}M^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{j})\beta^{(i)}_{r}dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(i)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{j})

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\quad+\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(i)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)\beta^{(j)}_{r}dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(j)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)\beta^{(i)}_{r}dr

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\Big[\Xi^{(i)}_{r}\beta^{(j)}_{r}\negthinspace+\negthinspace\Xi^{(j)}_{r}\beta^{(i)}_{r}\Big]dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(i)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{i})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\Big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(j)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\Big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{j}),\quad s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty).

So
M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{i})M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{j})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}(\alpha_{r})_{ij}dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{ij})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(i)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\big(\int_{t}^{r}\beta^{(j)}_{r^{\prime}}dr^{\prime}\big)dM^{t,\mu}_{r}(\psi_{j})
,

s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
is also an
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
local martingale, which implies that
the quadratic variation of the
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
local martingale

M^{t,\mu}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{1}),\cdots\negthinspace,M^{t,\mu}_{s}(\psi_{d+l})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Xi_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\beta_{r}dr
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
is

\big\langle M^{t,\mu},M^{t,\mu}\big\rangle_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\alpha_{r}dr
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.

Let
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,
a\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{l}
and set
\displaystyle{\cal H}^{a}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\binom{-\sigma^{T}\big(s,X_{s\land\cdot},\mu\big)a}{a}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)
.
The stochastic exponential of the
{\bf G}^{t,P}-
martingale

\big\{\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}{\cal H}^{a}_{r}\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace dM^{t,\mu}_{r}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}{\cal H}^{a}_{r}\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace dM^{t,\mu}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}({\cal H}^{a}_{r})^{T}\alpha_{r}{\cal H}^{a}_{r}dr\Big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\exp\Big\{\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}{\cal H}^{a}_{r}\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace d\Xi_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}{\cal H}^{a}_{r}\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace\beta_{r}dr\Big\}

\displaystyle\negthinspace=\negthinspace\exp\bigg\{a\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace\Big(\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}dX_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}\sigma\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dB_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}b\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\Big)\bigg\},\quad s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty).

Letting
a
vary over
\mathbb{R}^{l}
yields that
P-
a.s.,

X_{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(t)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}b\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\tau^{t}_{n}\land s}\sigma\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dB_{r}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.
Sending
n\negthinspace\to\negthinspace\infty
then renders that
P-
a.s.,

X_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(t)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}b\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\sigma\big(r,X_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)dB_{r}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.
Viewing SDE (
1.4
) with the control process
\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
\big(\Omega,{\cal F},{\bf G}^{t,P},P\big)
,
we know from Proposition
1.1
that there is a unique
\big\{{\cal G}^{t,P}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process satisfying (
1.4
). Hence,
P\{X_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s},\;\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
.
\Box

Proof of Proposition
1.3
: 1a)
Given a sigma-field
{\cal G}
of
\Omega_{0}
, define

\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal G})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{A\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Omega\negthinspace:\exists\,{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}
and
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
s.t.

{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A\}},\,\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}\big\}
.
As
{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in\Omega\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in\Omega\}}
for any
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega
,
it is clear that
\Omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
.
When
A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
, there exist
{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}
and
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
such that

{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A\}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
.
Then
{\cal A}^{c}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}
satisfies that

{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}^{c}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A^{c}\}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
,
so
A^{c}
also belongs to
\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
.
If
\{A_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
, for any
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}

there exist
{\cal A}_{k}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}
and
{\cal N}_{k}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
such that

{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}_{k}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A_{k}\}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{k}
. Then
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}{\cal A}_{k}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal G}
satisfies that

{\bf 1}_{\big\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}{\cal A}_{k}\big\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\prod}{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in{\cal A}_{k}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\prod}{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in A_{k}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\big\{\omega\in\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}A_{k}\big\}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}{\cal N}^{c}_{k}
,
which shows that
\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}A_{k}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
with
({\cal A},{\cal N})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cap}{\cal A}_{k},\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\overset{}{\cup}}{\cal N}_{k}\Big)
.
Hence
\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
is a sigma
-
field of
\Omega
.

Let
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
. For any
r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s]
and
{\cal E}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, since

\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(B^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})
iff
W^{t}_{r}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace W_{r}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\negthinspace-\negthinspace W_{t}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t}_{r}(\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal E}

iff
B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(W^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})
,
one has

{\bf 1}_{\{B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\in(W^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in(B^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})\}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega
, which means

(B^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s})
with
{\cal A}\negthinspace=\negthinspace(W^{t}_{r})^{-1}({\cal E})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s}
and
{\cal N}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\emptyset
. So
{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{s}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s})
.
When
s\negthinspace>\negthinspace t
, as
{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{r}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{r})\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s-})
for any

,
one further has

{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{s-}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sigma\Big(\underset{r\in[t,s)}{\cup}{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{r}\Big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}({\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s-})
.

1b)
Similar to
\mathscr{S}_{t,{\bf w}}\big({\cal G}\big)
in Part (1a),

\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{t,{\bf w}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{D\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega\negthinspace:\exists\,{\cal D}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{P}^{W^{t}}
and
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
s.t.
{\bf 1}_{\{(s,B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\in{\cal D}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega)\in D\}},\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}\big\}
is a sigma
-
field of
[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega
.

Set
\Lambda_{t}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\{t\}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace A_{o}\negthinspace:A_{o}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{t}\big\}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\big\{(s,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace A\negthinspace:s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\mathbb{Q},\,A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{s-}\big\}

{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-

-

. For any

and

,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
. We can deduce that

{\bf 1}_{\{(s,B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\in\{t\}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}_{o}\}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega)\in\{t\}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace A_{o}\}}
,

\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}

is of
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{t,{\bf w}}
with

.
For any

and

, one can find some
{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W^{t}}_{s-}

and
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
such that

,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
.

with
{\cal D}\negthinspace=\negthinspace(s,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}
.
Hence,
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{t,{\bf w}}
contains

and thus includes

.

1c)

Now, let
\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be a general
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
progressively measurable process on
\Omega
.
Similar to Lemma 2.4 of
[
38
]
, one can construct a
[0,1]-
valued
{\bf F}^{B^{t}}-
predictable process
\big\{\nu_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on

such that
\nu_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}\big(\mu_{s}(\omega)\big)
for

a.s.

Fubini Theorem yields that for all
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega
except on a

for a.e.

.
By Part (1b) and a standard approximation scheme, we can find a
[0,1]-
valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process

\big\{\nu^{o}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on

and an

Set
{\cal N}_{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal N}^{1}_{\nu}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace{\cal N}^{2}_{\nu}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})

, which is a

valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process. Given

, it holds for a.e.

and thus

.

2)
Let
{\bf x}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
and let
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. On

,

M^{t,\mu^{o}}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi\big(W^{t}_{s},\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{b}\big(r,\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{o}_{r}\big)\cdot D\varphi\big(W^{t}_{r},\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r}\big)dr\\
-\frac{1}{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{o}_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi(W^{t}_{r},\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r})dr
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

is an
{\bf F}^{W^{t},P_{0}}-
adapted continuous process
and
\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|(W^{t}_{s},\,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{s})\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)

As

is
the unique strong solution of SDE (

) on

,

\Big\{M^{t,\mu^{o}}_{s\land\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}}(\varphi)\Big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded

Since
W^{t}_{s}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t}_{s}(\omega)
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega
,
applying Lemma

with

,
(\Omega_{1},{\cal F}_{1},P_{1},B^{1})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B\big)
,
(\Omega_{2},{\cal F}_{2},P_{2},B^{2})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\Omega_{0},\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}),P_{0},W\big)
and
\Phi\negthinspace=\negthinspace B^{t,{\bf w}}
implies that

\breve{X}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{s}(B^{t,{\bf w}})
,
\breve{M}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(M^{t,\mu^{o}}_{s}(\varphi)\big)(B^{t,{\bf w}})
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

are
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
adapted continuous processes and
\breve{\tau}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}(B^{t,{\bf w}})
is an
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
stopping time.

Let
t\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s\negthinspace<\negthinspace r\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
and
\big\{(s_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\big\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[t,s]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
We can also deduce from Lemma
A.1
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\Big[\Big(M^{t,\mu^{o}}_{r\land\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace M^{t,\mu^{o}}_{s\land\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}}(\varphi)\Big){\bf 1}_{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}(W^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})}\Big]

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{\omega_{0}\in\Omega_{0}}\Big(\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(r\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}(\omega_{0}),\omega_{0}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}(\omega_{0}),\omega_{0}\big)\Big){\bf 1}_{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}\{W^{t}_{s_{i}}(\omega_{0})\in{\cal E}_{i}\}}(P\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace(B^{t,{\bf w}})^{-1})(d\omega_{0})

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{\omega\in\Omega}\Big(\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(r\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)),B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\tau^{t,\mu^{o}}_{n}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)),B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)\Big){\bf 1}_{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}\{W^{t}_{s_{i}}(B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\in{\cal E}_{i}\}}P(d\omega)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{\omega\in\Omega}\Big(\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(r\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\breve{\tau}_{n}(\omega),B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big({M^{t,\mu^{o}}}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\breve{\tau}_{n}(\omega),B^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)\Big){\bf 1}_{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}\{B^{t}_{s_{i}}(\omega)\in{\cal E}_{i}\}}P(d\omega)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{P}\Big[\Big(\breve{M}_{r\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\breve{M}_{s\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\Big){\bf 1}_{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}(B^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})}\Big].

So the Lambda-system
\breve{\Lambda}_{s,r}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{\infty}\negthinspace:E_{P}\big[\big(\breve{M}_{r\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\breve{M}_{s\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{A}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\big\}
includes
the Pi-system
\Big\{\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}(B^{t}_{s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})\negthinspace:\big\{(s_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\big\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[t,s]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\Big\}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{P}({\cal F}^{B^{t}}_{\infty})
.
An application of Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem (see e.g Theorem 3.2 of
[
7
]
) shows that

{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\breve{\Lambda}_{s,r}
, i.e.,
E_{P}\big[\big(\breve{M}_{r\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\breve{M}_{s\land\breve{\tau}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{A}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
for any
A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{B^{t},P}_{s}
.
To wit, the
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
adapted continuous process

\breve{M}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\varphi\big(B^{t}_{s},\breve{X}_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{b}\big(r,\breve{X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi\big(B^{t}_{r},\breve{X}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,\breve{X}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi(B^{t}_{r},\breve{X}_{r})dr
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
stopped by the
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
stopping time

\breve{\tau}_{n}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|(B^{t}_{s},\breve{X}_{s})\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)

is a bounded
{\bf F}^{B^{t},P}-
martingale. Using Part (i) of Proposition
1.2
yields that
P\{\breve{X}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{s},\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\}=1
.

Moreover, for any
\psi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{H}_{o}
, one has

E_{P}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{P}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}\big(r,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot}(B^{t,{\bf w}}),\mu^{o}_{r}(B^{t,{\bf w}})\big)dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\big[\int_{t}^{\infty}\negthinspace\psi^{-}(r,X^{t,{\bf x},\mu^{o}}_{r\land\cdot},\mu^{o}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
.
\Box

Proof of Theorem
3.1
:

Fix
(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}
and
(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\{y_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\{z_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Re\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Re
.

1)
We first show that
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
:
If
{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\emptyset
, then
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace-\infty\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
.

So we assume
{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset
and let
(\mu,\tau)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
.

1a)
Define a process
{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace t)\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t}_{s\vee t}(\omega)
,

\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}
.
According to Proposition
1.3
, there exists a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process
\widehat{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

on
\Omega_{0}
and an
{\cal N}_{\mu}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\mathfrak{p}}({\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t}}_{\infty})
such that for any
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\mu}
,

\mu_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}\big({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)
for a.e.
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)
.
By Fubini Theorem,

{\cal N}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}\negthinspace:\mu_{s}(\omega)\hbox{ is not Borel-measurable in }s\in[t,\infty)\big\}
is a
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}-
measurable
set with zero
\mathfrak{p}-
measure or
{\cal N}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{\mathfrak{p}}\big({\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t}}_{\infty}\big)
.
Then process

\widetilde{\mu}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big({\bf 1}_{\{s\in[0,t)\}}{\bf u}(s)\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{s\in[t,\infty)\}}\mu_{s}(\omega)\big){\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in{\cal N}^{c}\}}\negthinspace+\negthinspace u_{0}{\bf 1}_{\{\omega\in{\cal N}\}}
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}
satisfies that
\widetilde{\mu}_{\cdot}(\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
for any
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}
.

Let us simply set
\theta\negthinspace=\negthinspace(t,{\bf x},\mu)
. Define a mapping
\Psi\negthinspace:{\cal Q}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
by

\Psi(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega),\widetilde{\mu}_{\cdot}(\omega),{\cal X}^{\theta}(\omega),\tau(\omega)\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}
.
Since
{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s}(\Psi(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{s}(\Psi(\omega))\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{t}(\Psi(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{t}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t}_{s}(\omega)
,

\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}

and since
\big\{{\cal X}^{\theta}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}
is an
\big\{{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process,
we can deduce that the mapping
\Psi
is
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{s}\big/{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{s}-
measurable for any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
.

Let
\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)
.
The
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
progressive measurability of process
\big\{\widetilde{\mu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
implies that
process
\big\{\varphi(s,\widetilde{\mu}_{s})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is also
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
progressively measurable
and the random variable

I_{\varphi}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\cdot}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s,{\bf u}(s))ds\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t}^{\infty}\varphi(s,\widetilde{\mu}_{s}(\omega))ds
,
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}
is thus
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}-
measurable.
Lemma
1.3
(1) then renders that the mapping
\widetilde{\mu}_{\cdot}\negthinspace:{\cal Q}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
is
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}\big/\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})-
measurable,
which together with the
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}-
measurability of
{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}
,
{\cal X}^{\theta}
,
\tau
shows that
the mapping
\Psi
is also
{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}\big/\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})-
measurable.

Let
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
be the probability measure induced by
\Psi
, i.e.,

{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big(\Psi^{-1}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\big)
,
\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})
.

1b)
Set
\overline{\mu}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\overline{W}})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
, which is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
predictable process on
{\overline{\Omega}}
.
Since
{\cal N}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\mu}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}\negthinspace:\mu_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}\negthinspace:\widetilde{\mu}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}
,
we can deduce that

{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big\{{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\mu}_{s}\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{{\overline{U}}_{s}(\Psi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\overline{W}}(\Psi))\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}\negthinspace:\widetilde{\mu}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
. Namely,
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}
satisfies (D1) in the definition of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.

Fix
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. We define an
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
adapted continuous process

{\cal M}^{\theta}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi\big({\cal B}^{t}_{s},{\cal X}^{\theta}_{s}\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{b}\big(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi\big({\cal B}^{t}_{r},{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\negthinspace\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi({\cal B}^{t}_{r},{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r})dr
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

and define an
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
stopping time
\rule[4.12564pt]{5.97508pt}{0.7pt}\hskip-3.35pt\rule[-0.42677pt]{0.8pt}{5.12149pt}~^{\theta}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|({\cal B}^{t}_{s},{\cal X}^{\theta}_{s})\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)
.
Applying Proposition
1.2
with
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,X,\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p},{\cal B},{\cal X}^{\theta},\mu)
yields that

\big\{{\cal M}^{\theta}_{s\land\rule[3.69885pt]{4.55244pt}{0.6pt}\hskip-2.6pt\rule[0.28453pt]{0.7pt}{3.69885pt}\;^{\theta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

is a bounded
\big({\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}},\mathfrak{p}\big)-
martingale.

Since
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big\{{\overline{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{{\overline{X}}_{s}(\Psi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{{\cal X}^{\theta}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf x}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
, using Proposition
1.2

with
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,X,\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}),{\overline{P}}_{\Psi},{\overline{W}},{\overline{X}},\overline{\mu}\big)

shows that
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t}-
adapted continuous process under
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}
.
Given
\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\mu}
,
since
\overline{\mu}_{s}\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}\big({\overline{W}}(\Psi(\omega))\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}\big({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu_{s}(\omega)

for a.e.
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)
,
we see that

\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s}(\varphi)\big)\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\cal M}^{\theta}_{s}(\varphi)\big)(\omega)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
and

{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\rule[4.12564pt]{5.97508pt}{0.7pt}\hskip-3.35pt\rule[-0.42677pt]{0.8pt}{5.12149pt}~^{\theta}_{n}(\omega)
. Then

\displaystyle\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}(\Psi(\omega)),\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rule[4.12564pt]{5.97508pt}{0.7pt}\hskip-3.35pt\rule[-0.42677pt]{0.8pt}{5.12149pt}~^{\theta}_{n}(\omega),\Psi(\omega)\big)

(6.8)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big({\cal M}^{\theta}(\varphi)\big)\big(s\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rule[4.12564pt]{5.97508pt}{0.7pt}\hskip-3.35pt\rule[-0.42677pt]{0.8pt}{5.12149pt}~^{\theta}_{n}(\omega),\omega\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\cal M}^{\theta}_{s\land\rule[3.69885pt]{4.55244pt}{0.6pt}\hskip-2.6pt\rule[0.28453pt]{0.7pt}{3.69885pt}\;^{\theta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\omega),\quad\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}_{\mu}.

Let
t_{1},t_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
with
t_{1}\negthinspace<\negthinspace t_{2}
and let
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{t_{1}}
.
As
\Psi^{-1}({\overline{A}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{t_{1}}
, the
\big({\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}},\mathfrak{p}\big)-
martingality of

\big\{{\cal M}^{\theta}_{s\land\rule[3.69885pt]{4.55244pt}{0.6pt}\hskip-2.6pt\rule[0.28453pt]{0.7pt}{3.69885pt}\;^{\theta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
and (
6.8
) imply that

E_{{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{t_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{t_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{\overline{A}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\Big(\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{t_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\Psi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{t_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\Psi)\Big){\bf 1}_{\Psi^{-1}({\overline{A}})}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\big({\cal M}^{\theta}_{t_{2}\land\rule[3.69885pt]{4.55244pt}{0.6pt}\hskip-2.6pt\rule[0.28453pt]{0.7pt}{3.69885pt}\;^{\theta}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal M}^{\theta}_{t_{1}\land\rule[3.69885pt]{4.55244pt}{0.6pt}\hskip-2.6pt\rule[0.28453pt]{0.7pt}{3.69885pt}\;^{\theta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{\Psi^{-1}({\overline{A}})}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
So
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
\big({\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t},{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big)-
martingale.
By Remark
3.1
(ii),
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}
satisfies (D2)+(D3) in the definition of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.

1c)
Since
W^{t}_{s}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}(\omega))\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{t}(\omega)\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t}_{s}(\omega)
for any
(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega
,
taking
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,\Phi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p},{\cal B},{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}})
in Lemma
A.2
(2) shows that

\mathfrak{p}\big\{\tau\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\tau}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
for some
[t,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t},P_{0}}-
stopping time
\widehat{\tau}
on
\Omega_{0}
,
it follows that
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\tau}({\overline{W}})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{{\overline{T}}(\Psi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\tau}({\overline{W}}(\Psi))\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{\tau\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\tau}({\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
. Since
{\overline{W}}_{\negthinspace s}\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t,{\bf w}}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s)
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}

and since
{\overline{U}}_{s}\big(\Psi(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widetilde{\mu}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf u}(s)
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
,
it is clear that

{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\big\{{\overline{W}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t];{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf u}(s)\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,t)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{p}\big\{{\overline{W}}_{s}(\Psi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf w}(s),\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t];{\overline{U}}_{s}(\Psi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf u}(s)\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,t)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
. Thus
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}
.
For any
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle E_{{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)dr\bigg]

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{{\overline{T}}(\Psi)}g_{i}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot}(\Psi),{\overline{U}}_{r}(\Psi)\big)dr\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\widetilde{\mu}_{r})dr\Big]

(6.9)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\tau}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i}

and similarly

E_{{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i}
,
which means that
{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)
.
Then an analogy to (
6.9
) renders that

E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\tau}f(r,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{r\land\cdot},\mu_{r})dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\tau<\infty\}}\pi\big(\tau,{\cal X}^{\theta}_{\tau\land\cdot}\big)\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{{\overline{P}}_{\Psi}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}f\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}<\infty\}}\pi\big({\overline{T}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{T}}\land\cdot}\big)\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
.
Taking supremum over
(\mu,\tau)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
yields
V(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)
.

2)
As
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
, we automatically have
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf u},{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)
.
It remains to demonstrate that
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
.
If
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\emptyset
, then
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace=\negthinspace-\infty\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
.

Assume
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\emptyset
and let
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
.
By (D1) of Definition
3.1
, there exists a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process

\widehat{\nu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\nu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that

{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{U}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\nu}_{s}\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
,
where
\overline{\nu}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\nu}_{s}\big({\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)
,
\,\forall\,(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
predictable process on
{\overline{\Omega}}
.

Set
\vartheta\negthinspace=\negthinspace(t,{\bf x},\overline{\nu})
.
Given
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,

{\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace s},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace s})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{b}\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot},\overline{\nu}_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace r},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r})dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot},\overline{\nu}_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi({\overline{W}}^{t}_{\negthinspace r},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r})dr
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
is an
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}-
adapted continuous process
and
{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|({\overline{W}}^{t}_{s},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{s})\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)

is an
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}-
stopping time. Since
{\overline{W}}^{t}
is a Brownian motion under
{\overline{P}}
by (D2) of Definition
3.1
,
applying Proposition
1.2
with
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,X,\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}),{\overline{P}},{\overline{W}},\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta},\overline{\nu}\big)
shows that

\displaystyle\big\{{\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}\hbox{ is a bounded ${\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}-$martingale.}

(6.10)

Let
(\mathfrak{u}_{o},\mathfrak{x}_{o},\mathfrak{t}_{o})
be an arbitrary triplet in
\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{\overset{}{X}}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace[t,\infty]
and define a mapping
\Psi_{o}\negthinspace:{\cal Q}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
by

\Psi_{o}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big({\cal B}(\omega),\mathfrak{u}_{o},\mathfrak{x}_{o},\mathfrak{t}_{o}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
,
\,\forall\,\omega\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal Q}
.
(Actually, we are indifferent to the second, third and fourth components of
\Psi_{o}(\omega)

Since
{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s}(\Psi_{o}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{s}(\Psi_{o}(\omega))\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{t}(\Psi_{o}(\omega))\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal B}^{t}_{s}(\omega)
for any

,
applying Lemma
A.1
with
t_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace t
,
(\Omega_{1},{\cal F}_{1},P_{1},B^{1})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p},{\cal B}\big)
,
(\Omega_{2},{\cal F}_{2},P_{2},B^{2})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}),{\overline{P}},{\overline{W}}\big)
and
\Phi\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Psi_{o}
yields that

\displaystyle\Psi^{-1}_{o}\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{s}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t}}_{s},~\Psi^{-1}_{o}\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{s}\big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{s},~\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty]\quad\hbox{and}\quad\big(\mathfrak{p}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\Psi_{o}^{-1}\big)({\overline{A}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}({\overline{A}}),~\;\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}.

(6.11)

Then
\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace s}(\Psi_{o}({\overline{\omega}}))
,
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
defines an
\big\{{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}_{s\vee t}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process.

Set
\nu_{s}(\omega)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\nu}_{s}\big({\cal B}(\omega)\big)
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}
, which is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t}}-
predictable process on
{\cal Q}
. Let
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. We define an
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
adapted continuous process

\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\varphi({\cal B}^{t}_{s},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace s})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}\overline{b}\big(r,\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot},\nu_{r}\big)\negthinspace\cdot\negthinspace D\varphi({\cal B}^{t}_{r},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r})dr\negthinspace-\negthinspace\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\overline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}^{T}\big(r,\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot},\nu_{r}\big)\negthinspace:\negthinspace D^{2}\varphi({\cal B}^{t}_{r},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r})dr
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
and define an
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
stopping time
\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\inf\big\{s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace:\big|({\cal B}^{t}_{s},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{s})\big|\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace n\big\}\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace n)
.
Since
\overline{\nu}_{s}(\Psi_{o})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\nu}_{s}\big({\overline{W}}(\Psi_{o})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\nu}_{s}({\cal B})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\nu_{s}
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
,
applying Proposition
1.2
with
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,X,\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p},{\cal B},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta},\nu\big)
and using an analogy to (
6.8
) yield that

\big\{\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{s\land\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
adapted continuous process under
\mathfrak{p}
satisfying

\displaystyle\big({\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)\big(\Psi_{o}(\omega)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{s\land\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\omega),\quad\,\forall\,(s,\omega)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal Q}.

(6.12)

Let
t_{1},t_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
with
t_{1}\negthinspace<\negthinspace t_{2}
and let
A\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\cal B}^{t}}_{t_{1}}
.
Since
\Psi_{o}^{-1}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace A
for some
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t_{1}}
by (

),
we can derive from (

), (

) and (

) that

0\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{t_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{t_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{\overline{A}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\Big(\big({\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{t_{2}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\Psi_{o})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\big({\overline{{\cal M}}}^{\vartheta}_{t_{1}\land{\overline{\tau}}^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big)(\Psi_{o})\Big){\bf 1}_{\Psi_{o}^{-1}({\overline{A}})}\Big]\\
\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\big(\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{t_{2}\land\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{t_{1}\land\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big){\bf 1}_{A}\Big]
,
which implies that
\big\{\mathscr{M}^{\vartheta}_{s\land\zeta^{\vartheta}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
martingale.
Then an application of Proposition
1.2
with
(\Omega,{\cal F},P,B,X,\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\cal Q},{\cal F},\mathfrak{p},{\cal B},\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta},\nu\big)
shows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}\{\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\nu}_{s},~\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1.

(6.13)

By (D4) of Definition
3.1
,
there exists a
[t,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{W^{t},P_{0}}-
stopping time
\widehat{\gamma}
on
\Omega_{0}

such that
{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\gamma}({\overline{W}})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
.
Lemma
A.2
(1) renders that
\gamma\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\gamma}({\cal B})
is an
{\bf F}^{{\cal B}^{t},\mathfrak{p}}-
stopping time on
{\cal Q}

while
\widehat{\gamma}({\overline{W}})
is an
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}-
stopping time on
{\overline{\Omega}}
.
For any
i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, we can deduce from (D3), (
6.11
) and (

) that

\displaystyle y_{i}

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)dr\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\widehat{\gamma}({\overline{W}})}g_{i}\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot},\widehat{\nu}_{r}({\overline{W}})\big)dr\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\widehat{\gamma}({\overline{W}}(\Psi_{o}))}g_{i}\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\vartheta}_{\negthinspace r\land\cdot}(\Psi_{o}),\widehat{\nu}_{r}({\overline{W}}(\Psi_{o}))\big)dr\bigg]

(6.14)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\widehat{\gamma}({\cal B})}g_{i}(r,\mathscr{X}^{\vartheta}_{r\land\cdot},\widehat{\nu}_{r}({\cal B}))dr\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\gamma}g_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\nu}_{r\land\cdot},\nu_{r})dr\bigg],

and similarly
E_{\mathfrak{p}}\big[\int_{t}^{\gamma}h_{i}(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\nu}_{r\land\cdot},\nu_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i}
.
So
(\nu,\gamma)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal C}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
. Analogous to (
