),

\displaystyle\quad E_{\overline{P}}\bigg[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}f\big(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{T}}<\infty\}}\pi\big({\overline{T}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{T}}\land\cdot}\big)\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\mathfrak{p}}\Big[\int_{t}^{\gamma}f\big(r,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\nu}_{r\land\cdot},\nu_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\gamma<\infty\}}\pi\big(\gamma,{\cal X}^{t,{\bf x},\nu}_{\gamma\land\cdot}\big)\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z).

Taking supremum over
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
yields that
{\overline{V}}(t,{\bf x},y,z)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace V(t,{\bf x},y,z)
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
4.1
:

Lemma 4.1 of
[
4
]
shows that
\big(\mathfrak{S},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}\big)
is a complete separable metric space.
so we only need to verify that
\big(\mathfrak{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big)
is a complete separable metric space.

1)

Let
\mu^{1},\mu^{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
with
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
For any
n,k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,
the set
A^{n}_{k}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,n]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0}),\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)\geq 1/k\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}[0,n]\negthinspace\otimes\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-n}}{k}(ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0})(A^{n}_{k})\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{n}\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{2}_{s})\geq 1/k\}}e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{2}_{s})\big)ds\bigg]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{2}_{s})\big)ds\bigg]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0,

and thus
(ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0})(A^{n}_{k})\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
. As

A\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n,k\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}A^{n}_{k}
,
we see that
(ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0})(A)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
, i.e.,
\mu^{1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{2}
in
\mathfrak{U}
.
Also, For
\mu^{1},\mu^{2},\mu^{3}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
, (
6.1
) implies that

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{1},\mu^{3})\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{3}_{s})\big)ds\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace E_{P_{0}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{1}_{s},\mu^{2}_{s})\negthinspace+\negthinspace 1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{2}_{s},\mu^{3}_{s})\big)ds\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{2},\mu^{3})
.
So
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}
is a metric on
\mathfrak{U}
.

2)

Let
\{\mu^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\big(\mathfrak{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big)
.
We can find a subsequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
such that

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big(\mu^{n_{k}},\mu^{n_{k+1}}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2^{-k}
,
\,\forall\,k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
Fix
k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
Since an analogy to (
6.2
) shows that for any
\ell\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,

1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{n_{k}}_{s},\mu^{n_{k+\ell}}_{s})\Big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{n_{k+i-1}}_{s},\mu^{n_{k+i}}_{s})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
,
the monotone convergence theorem implies

E_{P_{0}}\Big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\Big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{n_{k}}_{s},\mu^{n_{k+\ell}}_{s})\Big)\Big)ds\Big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big(\mu^{n_{k+i-1}},\mu^{n_{k+i}}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-k-i+1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 2^{-k+1}
.
So
\big\{n_{k}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
has a subsequence
\big\{n_{k_{m}}\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\Big(\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\\
\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0}),\mu^{n_{k_{m}+\ell}}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0

for all
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
except on a
ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0}-
null set
{\cal N}
.
Given
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
, one has

\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\bigg(\underset{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0}),\mu^{n_{k_{j+m}}}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\bigg)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0

or
\big\{\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0})\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{U}
.
Let
\mu^{o}(s,\omega_{0})
be the limit of
\big\{\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0})\big\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\big(\mathbb{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big)
.

Let
\mathscr{P}^{W,P_{0}}
denote the predictable sigma-field on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
with respect to filtration
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}

(
i.e., the sigma-field of
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
generated by all
\big\{{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{s-}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted, càglàd processes
)
.
Define
\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\mathop{\underline{\rm lim}}}\mathscr{I}\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,1]
,
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
,
which is a
\mathscr{P}^{W,P_{0}}-
measurable function on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\big){\bf 1}_{\{\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\in\mathfrak{E}\}}\negthinspace+\negthinspace u_{0}{\bf 1}_{\{\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\notin\mathfrak{E}\}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{U},\quad\,\forall\,(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}

is also
\mathscr{P}^{W,P_{0}}-
measurable or the process
\{\mu^{*}_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
predictable
process on
\Omega_{0}
. To wit,
\mu^{*}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
.

For
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal N}^{c}
, the continuity of mapping
\mathscr{I}
shows

\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\mathscr{I}\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}\big(\mu^{o}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{E}
,
so
\mu^{*}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\underline{\mu}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{o}(s,\omega_{0})
.
The dominated convergence theorem yields that
\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}},\mu^{*}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{m\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{\omega_{o}\in\Omega_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{0})\in{\cal N}^{c}\}}\Big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mu^{n_{k_{m}}}(s,\omega_{0}),\mu^{o}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\Big)dsP_{0}(d\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
Similar to (
6.3
), one can further deduce that

\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big(\mu^{n},\mu^{*}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
Hence,
\mu^{*}
is a limit of
\{\mu^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\big(\mathfrak{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big)
.

3)

Let
{\cal L}^{0}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace L^{0}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0};\mathbb{U}\big)
be
the equivalence classes of all
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})-
measurable
functions
\mathfrak{X}
on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
in the sense that

\mathfrak{X}^{1},\mathfrak{X}^{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal L}^{0}
are equivalent if

\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mathfrak{X}^{1}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mathfrak{X}^{2}(s,\omega_{0})\}
is a
ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0}-
null set.
We endow
{\cal L}^{0}
with a metric

\displaystyle\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}(\mathfrak{X}^{1},\mathfrak{X}^{2})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{\omega_{0}\in\Omega_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{1}(s,\omega_{0}),\mathfrak{X}^{2}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\big)dsP_{0}(d\omega_{0}),\quad\,\forall\,\mathfrak{X}^{1},\mathfrak{X}^{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal L}^{0},

and we shall demonstrate that
\big({\cal L}^{0},\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}\big)
is a separable space.

Let
\{u_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable dense subset of the complete metric space
\big(\mathbb{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big)
.
Since both
[0,\infty)
and
\Omega_{0}
are separable metric spaces (and thus second-countable spaces),

[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
is also second-countable or it has a countable base
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let us enumerate the
2^{n}
elements of
\big\{\underset{i\in I}{\cup}O_{i}\negthinspace:I\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\{1,\cdots,n\}\big\}
by
\big\{\breve{O}^{n}_{1},\cdots,\breve{O}^{n}_{2^{n}}\big\}
and consider
the following countable collections of
\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})/\mathscr{B}[-1,1]-
measurable functions on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{\mathscr{I}(u_{j}){\bf 1}_{\breve{O}^{n}_{i}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{(\breve{O}^{n}_{i})^{c}}\negthinspace:i\in\{1,\cdots,2^{n}\},~j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\Big\},\quad\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{\underset{i=1,\cdots,k}{\max}\mathfrak{X}^{i}\negthinspace:k\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N},~\{\mathfrak{X}^{1},\cdots,\mathfrak{X}^{k}\}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathscr{C}_{n}\Big\}.

Clearly, each
\mathfrak{X}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}
takes values in a finite subset of
\big\{\mathscr{I}(u_{i})\big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\cup\{-1\}
.
By additionally assigning
\mathscr{I}^{-1}(-1)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace u_{0}
, one has

\widetilde{{\bf C}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\mathscr{I}^{-1}(\mathfrak{X})\negthinspace:\mathfrak{X}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}_{n}\hbox{ for some }n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}\big\}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big\{\hbox{$\{u_{i}\}^{\infty}_{i=0}-$valued $\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})-$measurable functions}\big\}\negthinspace=:\negthinspace\widehat{{\bf C}}
.

3a)
We claim that

\widehat{{\bf C}}
is a dense subset of
{\cal L}^{0}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}
:
For any
i,n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, we set
o^{n}_{i}
and
\widetilde{o}^{n}_{i}
as in the proof of Lemma
1.2
.
Given
\mathfrak{X}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal L}^{0}
and
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,
define a member of
\widehat{{\bf C}}
by
\mathfrak{X}^{n}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{0})\in A^{n}_{i}\}}u_{i}
,

(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
, where

A^{n}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{o}^{n}_{i}\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})
. Since

\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{n}(s,\omega_{0}),\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{0})\in A^{n}_{i}\}}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0}),u_{i}\big)\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2^{-n}
,
\,\forall\,(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
,
one can deduce that
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{n},\mathfrak{X}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{\omega_{0}\in\Omega_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{n}(s,\omega_{0}),\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\big)dsP_{0}(d\omega_{0})\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace 2^{-n}
.
So
\widehat{{\bf C}}
is a dense subset of
{\cal L}^{0}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}
.

3b)
We next show that the countable set
\widetilde{{\bf C}}
is dense in
\widehat{{\bf C}}
and is thus dense in
{\cal L}^{0}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}
:
Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widehat{{\bf C}}
and
\varepsilon\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
.
We set
\widehat{A}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{i}\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})
,
\,\forall\,i\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
.
Since
P(A)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{\Omega_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{0})\in A\}}dsP_{0}(d\omega_{0})

is a probability measure on
\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0},\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})\big)
, there exists
N\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that

P\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)<\varepsilon/3
.

Given
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,N
, Proposition 7.17 of
[
6
]
shows that

P({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\widehat{A}_{i})\negthinspace<\negthinspace\frac{\varepsilon}{3N}
for some open subset
{\cal O}_{i}
of
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
containing
\widehat{A}_{i}
.
Since
{\cal O}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}O_{\ell^{i}_{n}}
for some subsequence
\big\{\ell^{i}_{n}\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

of
\mathbb{N}
, we can find
n_{i}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that

P\big({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)<\frac{\varepsilon}{3N}
,
where
\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{n=1}{\overset{n_{i}}{\cup}}O_{\ell^{i}_{n}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})
.
As
\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\big\{\breve{O}^{\mathfrak{n}}_{j}\big\}^{2^{\mathfrak{n}}}_{j=1}
for
\mathfrak{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{i=1,\cdots,N}{\max}\ell^{i}_{n_{i}}
,
we see that
\mathfrak{X}^{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}(u_{i}){\bf 1}_{\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\breve{{\cal O}}^{c}_{i}}
belongs to
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
Define
\mathfrak{X}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\Big(\underset{i=1,\cdots,N}{\max}\,\mathfrak{X}^{i}\Big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widetilde{{\bf C}}
.

For
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,N
,
if
{\cal A}_{i}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\widehat{A}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)\Big\backslash\Big(\underset{j\leq N;j\neq i}{\cup}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0})
is not empty,
it holds for any
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}_{i}
that

\mathfrak{X}^{j}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{j=i\}}\mathscr{I}(u_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{j\neq i\}}
for
j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{1,\cdots,N\}

and thus
\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{i}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}(s,\omega_{0})
.
Also, if
{\cal A}_{0}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)^{c}

is not empty, it holds for any
(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}_{0}
that
\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathscr{I}^{-1}(-1)\negthinspace=\negthinspace u_{0}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}(s,\omega_{0})
.
Then

\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}\big(\mathfrak{X},\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{\omega_{0}\in\Omega_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{(s,\omega_{0})\in{\cal A}\}}e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}\big(\mathfrak{X}(s,\omega_{0}),\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}(s,\omega_{0})\big)\big)dsP_{0}(d\omega_{0})

for
{\cal A}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=0}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}(\widehat{A}_{i}\backslash{\cal A}_{i})\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\bigg(\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\Big)\bigg)
.
Since

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{i}\backslash{\cal A}_{i}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\widehat{A}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}^{c}_{i}\big)\hbox{$\bigcup$}\Big(\underset{j\leq N;j\neq i}{\cup}\big(\widehat{A}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\big)\Big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big({\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\big)\hbox{$\bigcup$}\Big(\underset{j\neq i}{\cup}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\backslash\widehat{A}_{j}\Big),\quad i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,N

and since
\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\bigcap\Big(\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\Big(\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)^{c}\Big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\big(\breve{{\cal O}}_{j}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\widehat{A}^{c}_{j}\big)\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\underset{j=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}\big({\cal O}_{j}\backslash\widehat{A}_{j}\big)
,
we can deduce that

{\cal A}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal O}_{i}\backslash\breve{{\cal O}}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cup}}{\cal O}_{i}\backslash\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)\bigcup\Big(\underset{i>N}{\cup}\widehat{A}_{i}\Big)
.
It follows that
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}\big(\mathfrak{X},\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace P({\cal A})\negthinspace<\negthinspace\varepsilon
.
Namely, the countable collection
\widetilde{{\bf C}}
is dense in
\widehat{{\bf C}}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{{\cal L}^{0}}}
.

Viewed as a subset of
{\cal L}^{0}
,
\mathfrak{U}
is also separable under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}
.
Therefore,
\big(\mathfrak{U},\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}\big)
is a complete separable space.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
4.2
: 1)

We first show that
\Gamma
is injective:
Set
\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(\mathbb{Q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace[0,\pi/2)\big)\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\{\pi/2\}

and let
(\mu^{1},\tau_{1}),(\mu^{2},\tau_{2})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}\times\mathfrak{S}
such that
\Gamma(\mu^{1},\tau_{1})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Gamma(\mu^{2},\tau_{2})
.

Let
q\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}
and
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. We set
{\cal E}^{q}_{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(q\negthinspace-\negthinspace 1/n,q\negthinspace+\negthinspace 1/n)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace[0,\pi/2]
.
Also let
k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,2
. We know from (
1.1
) and Lemma
A.3
that
A^{i}_{n,k,j,q}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace(\mu^{i})^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\big)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\arctan(\tau_{i})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal E}^{q}_{n}\big\}

belongs to
{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}
.
Then
A_{n,k,j,q}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace A^{1}_{n,k,j,q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace(A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}
satisfies that

\displaystyle P_{0}(A_{n,k,j,q})

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace P_{0}\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}:\mu^{1}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big),\tau_{1}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\tan({\cal E}^{q}_{n})\big\}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big(\Gamma(\mu^{1},\tau_{1})\big)\big((A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\tan({\cal E}^{q}_{n})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\Gamma(\mu^{2},\tau_{2})\big)\big((A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\tan({\cal E}^{q}_{n})\big)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace P_{0}\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}:\mu^{2}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big),\tau_{2}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\tan({\cal E}^{q}_{n})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\big((A^{2}_{n,k,j,q})^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace A^{2}_{n,k,j,q}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0.

We claim that

{\cal A}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu^{1}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\big\}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\tau_{1}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\tau_{2}(\omega_{0})\big\}
is equal to

\underset{n,k,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\,\underset{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}}{\overset{}{\cup}}A_{n,k,j,q}
.

(6.16)

Clearly,
\underset{n,k,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\,\underset{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}}{\overset{}{\cup}}A_{n,k,j,q}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\cal A}
.
Assume that
{\cal A}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\Big(\underset{n,k,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\cup}\,\underset{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}}{\overset{}{\cup}}A_{n,k,j,q}\Big)^{c}

is not empty and has an element
\omega_{0}
.

Given
n,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
, since the proof of Lemma
1.1
selected
\{\mathfrak{m}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
as a countable dense subset
of the topological space
\big(\mathfrak{P}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}),\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}\big(\mathfrak{P}([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U})\big)\big)
,
there exist
\mathfrak{k}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{k}(n,j)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and
\mathfrak{q}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mathfrak{q}(n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}_{\pi}
such that

\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mu^{1}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{k}},\phi_{j})

and
\arctan\big(\tau_{1}(\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal E}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}
.
This shows
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(\mu^{1})^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{k}},\phi_{j})\big)\big)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\arctan(\tau_{1})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal E}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace A^{1}_{n,\mathfrak{k},j,\mathfrak{q}}
.
Since
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A^{c}_{n,\mathfrak{k},j,\mathfrak{q}}
, we see that
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A^{2}_{n,\mathfrak{k},j,\mathfrak{q}}
, i.e.,

\big(\mu^{2}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0}),\arctan(\tau_{2}(\omega_{0}))\big)
also belongs to
\mathfrak{i}^{-1}_{\mathbb{J}}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{k}},\phi_{j})\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}^{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}
. It follows that

\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\big[\phi_{j}\big(s,\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\phi_{j}\big(s,\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)\big]ds\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2/n
and
\rho_{\overset{}{+}}\big(\tau_{1}(\omega_{0}),\tau_{2}(\omega_{0})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big|\arctan(\tau_{1}(\omega_{0}))\negthinspace-\negthinspace\arctan(\tau_{2}(\omega_{0}))\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace 2/n
.
Letting
n\negthinspace\to\negthinspace\infty
yields that
\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\phi_{j}\big(s,\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\phi_{j}\big(s,\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)ds
and
\tau_{1}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\tau_{2}(\omega_{0})
.

As
\{\phi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is dense in
\widehat{C}_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
by Proposition 7.20 of
[
6
]
,
the dominated convergence theorem implies that

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\,\phi\big(s,\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)ds\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}\,\phi\big(s,\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big)ds

holds for any
\phi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\widehat{C}_{b}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)
.
By a standard approximation, this equality also holds for any bounded Borel-measurable functions
\phi
on
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}
.
For any
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)
, taking
\phi(r,u)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{r\in[0,s]\}}\mathscr{I}(u)
gives that

\int_{0}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\mu^{1}_{r}(\omega_{0})\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{0}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\mu^{2}_{r}(\omega_{0})\big)dr
.
Then we obtain that
\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})
for a.e.
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)

or
\mu^{1}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})
in
\mathbb{J}
. A contradiction appears.
So the claim (
6.16
) holds.

By (
6.15
), one has
P_{0}\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu^{1}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\tau_{1}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\tau_{2}(\omega_{0})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
. The former together with Fubini Theorem renders that
(ds\negthinspace\times\negthinspace dP_{0})\big\{(s,\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\mu^{1}_{s}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\mu^{2}_{s}(\omega_{0})\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
or
\mu^{1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\mu^{2}
in
\mathfrak{U}
, while the latter directly means
\tau_{1}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\tau_{2}
in
\mathfrak{S}
.
Hence, the mapping
\Gamma\negthinspace:\mathfrak{U}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)

is injective.

2)
We next discuss the continuity of
\Gamma
:
Let
\{\mu^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathfrak{U}
converging to a
\mu\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{U}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}

and let
\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathfrak{S}
converging to a
\tau\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{S}
under
\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}

(
i.e.,
\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{n},\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}(\tau_{n},\tau)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
)
.
We need to demonstrate that
P^{n}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Gamma(\mu^{n},\tau_{n})
converges to
P\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Gamma(\mu,\tau)
under
the weak topology of
\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})P^{n}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})P\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\big)

(6.17)

for any bounded continuous function
\psi\negthinspace:\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
.

Let
\psi
be a real-valued bounded continuous function on
\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}
.
For (

), it suffices to show that for any subsequence
\big\{\big(\mu^{n_{k}},\tau_{n_{k}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of

\big\{(\mu^{n},\tau_{n})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, we can find a subsequence
\big\{\big(\mu^{n^{\prime}_{k}},\tau_{n^{\prime}_{k}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

that satisfies (

).

Let
\big\{\big(\mu^{n_{k}},\tau_{n_{k}}\big)\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be an arbitrary subsequence of
\big\{(\mu^{n},\tau_{n})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Since
0\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mu^{n_{k}},\mu)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}E_{P_{0}}\big[\int_{0}^{\infty}\negthinspace e^{-s}\big(1\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\rho_{\overset{}{\mathbb{U}}}(\mu^{n_{k}}_{s},\mu_{s})\big)ds\big]
,

of

for all
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
except on a

for a.e.

. For any

.
Namely,
\Big\{\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}\big(\mu^{\widetilde{n}_{k}}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\big)\Big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}

\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)\big)

of
\mathfrak{P}\big([0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U}\big)

converges to
\mu_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})
under the induced topology

of
\mathbb{J}
.

As
\displaystyle 0\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\rho_{\overset{}{\mathfrak{S}}}\big(\tau_{\overset{}{\widetilde{n}_{k}}},\tau\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}E_{P_{0}}\big[\rho_{\overset{}{+}}(\tau_{\overset{}{\widetilde{n}_{k}}},\tau)\big]
, one can extract a subsequence

from

for all
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}
except on a

null set

.
Given
\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace({\cal N}_{1}\negthinspace\cup\negthinspace{\cal N}_{2})^{c}

also converges to

under
\mathfrak{T}_{\sharp}(\mathbb{J})
,
the continuity of

and the bounded convergence theorem yield that

\displaystyle\hskip-39.83368pt\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})P^{n^{\prime}_{k}}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{k\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{\Omega_{0}}\psi\Big(\omega_{0},\mu^{n^{\prime}_{k}}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0}),\tau_{n^{\prime}_{k}}(\omega_{0})\Big)P_{0}(d\omega_{0})

\displaystyle\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{\Omega_{0}}\psi\big(\omega_{0},\mu_{\cdot}(\omega_{0}),\tau(\omega_{0})\big)P_{0}(d\omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})P\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\mathfrak{t})\big).\hskip 85.35826pt\hbox{\hfill$\Box$ \vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt}

Proof of Lemma
4.3
:

Let
\{t_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,\infty)
converge to
t\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)

and let
\{{\overline{P}}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
converge to
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
under
the weak topology of
\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)

(
i.e.,
\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\phi({\overline{\omega}}){\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\phi({\overline{\omega}}){\overline{P}}(d{\overline{\omega}})
for any bounded continuous function
\phi\negthinspace:{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
)
.
To see that

\big\{{\overline{Q}}_{t_{n},{\overline{P}}_{n}}\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}

under the weak topology of
\mathfrak{P}\big(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\big)

we let
\psi\negthinspace:\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}
be a bounded continuous function and have to show that

\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda){\overline{Q}}_{t_{n},{\overline{P}}_{n}}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda){\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\big)
.

Set
\|\psi\|_{\infty}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\underset{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}{\sup}\,\big|\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\big|

and let
\varepsilon\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
. Since the weakly convergent sequence
\{{\overline{P}}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
,
Prohorov’s Theorem yields that
\{{\overline{P}}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight, i.e.,
\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\,{\overline{P}}_{n}\big(\overline{{\cal K}}^{c}_{\varepsilon}\big)\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\frac{\varepsilon}{4\|\psi\|_{\infty}}
for some compact subset
\overline{{\cal K}}_{\varepsilon}
of
{\overline{\Omega}}
.

Since
{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\big({\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{U}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)
is a continuous mapping from
{\overline{\Omega}}
to
\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}
,
Lemma
A.4
implies that

\displaystyle\overline{\Phi}(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{s}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{s}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{T}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace s\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\mathscr{W}^{s}({\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}})),\mathscr{U}^{s}({\overline{U}}({\overline{\omega}})),{\overline{T}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace s\big),\quad\,\forall\,(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}

is a continuous mapping from
[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
to
\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T}
. There exists
\delta\negthinspace=\negthinspace\delta(t,\varepsilon)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,1)
such that

\big|\psi\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\overline{\Phi}(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\psi\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\overline{\Phi}(s^{\prime},{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\big|\\
<\negthinspace\varepsilon/4
for any
(s,{\overline{\omega}}),(s^{\prime},{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t\negthinspace+\negthinspace 1]\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\overline{{\cal K}}_{\varepsilon}
with
|s\negthinspace-\negthinspace s^{\prime}|\vee\rho_{\overset{}{{\overline{\Omega}}}}({\overline{\omega}},{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace<\negthinspace\delta
.
And we can find
N\negthinspace=\negthinspace N(t,\varepsilon)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
such that

\big|\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\psi\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}}){\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\psi\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}}){\overline{P}}(d{\overline{\omega}})\big|\negthinspace<\negthinspace\frac{\varepsilon}{4}

.

For any

, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\bigg|\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda){\overline{Q}}_{t_{n},{\overline{P}}_{n}}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\in\Omega_{0}\times\mathbb{J}\times\mathbb{T}}\psi(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda){\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\big(d(\omega_{0},\mathfrak{u},\lambda)\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\bigg|\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\Big(\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t_{n},{\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}})\big)\Big){\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\bigg|\negthinspace+\negthinspace\bigg|\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}})\big){\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace-\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{\Omega}}}\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}})\big){\overline{P}}(d{\overline{\omega}})\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace<\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in\overline{{\cal K}}_{\varepsilon}}\Big|\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t_{n},{\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}})\big)\Big|{\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in\overline{{\cal K}}^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\Big|\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t_{n},{\overline{\omega}})\big)\Big|{\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in\overline{{\cal K}}^{c}_{\varepsilon}}\Big|\psi\big(\overline{\Phi}(t,{\overline{\omega}})\big)\Big|{\overline{P}}_{n}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace+\negthinspace\varepsilon/4

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91684pt\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\frac{\varepsilon}{4}{\overline{P}}_{n}(\overline{{\cal K}}_{\varepsilon})\negthinspace+\negthinspace 2\|\psi\|_{\infty}{\overline{P}}_{n}\big(\overline{{\cal K}}^{c}_{\varepsilon}\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\varepsilon/4\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace\varepsilon.\hskip 298.75394pt\hbox{\hfill$\Box$ \vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt}

Proof of Proposition
4.1
:

.

1)

{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}

.
By (D1
′

\breve{\mu}_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\ddot{\mu}_{\mathfrak{s}}{\bf 1}_{\ddot{{\cal N}}^{c}}\negthinspace+\negthinspace u_{0}{\bf 1}_{\ddot{{\cal N}}}
,
\mathfrak{s}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)

As

by (D2),

\Phi\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}
yields that

\displaystyle\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\big)^{-1}(A_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\quad\hbox{and}\quad{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\big)^{-1}(A_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}(A_{0}),\quad\,\forall\,A_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}.

(6.18)

For any
A_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W}_{\infty}
,
{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
and
{\cal E}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{T})
,
since
\breve{\mu}^{-1}({\cal A})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}
by Lemma
A.3
and since
\ddot{\tau}^{-1}({\cal E})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}
,
we can derive that

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}(A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t},{\overline{T}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace t\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\ddot{\mu}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}),\ddot{\tau}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}\big\}

\displaystyle\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\breve{\mu}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}),\ddot{\tau}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})^{-1}\big\{(W,\breve{\mu},\ddot{\tau})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})^{-1}\big(A_{0}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{\mu}^{-1}({\cal A})\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\ddot{\tau}^{-1}({\cal E})\big)

\displaystyle\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\big(A_{0}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\breve{\mu}^{-1}({\cal A})\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\ddot{\tau}^{-1}({\cal E})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\big\{(W,\breve{\mu},\ddot{\tau})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big(\Gamma(\breve{\mu},\ddot{\tau})\big)(A_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal E}).

Then Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem implies that

{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Gamma(\breve{\mu},\ddot{\tau})
on
\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T})
.
So
{\overline{P}}
belongs to
{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{2}_{t}
.

Let
\widehat{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
be the
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process in (D1) such that the complement of
{\overline{\Omega}}_{\mu}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:{\overline{U}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\mu}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\hbox{ for a.e. }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}
is of
\mathscr{N}_{\overline{P}}\big(\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})\big)
, where
\overline{\mu}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}\big({\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)
,
\,\forall\,(s,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
.
Given
(\varphi,n)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
,
(D2
′
) of Remark
3.1
shows that
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\varphi)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
is a bounded
({\overline{{\bf F}}}^{t},{\overline{P}})-
martingale.
For any
(\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}^{2,<}_{+}
and
\{(s_{i},{\cal O}_{i})\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big(\mathbb{Q}\cap[0,\mathfrak{s}]\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
,
since
{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\varphi)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{s}(\varphi)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
on
{\overline{\Omega}}_{\mu}
and since
\big\{({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}},{\overline{X}}_{t+s_{i}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal O}_{i}\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}
for
i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots\negthinspace,k
,
one has
E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\varphi)\big)\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\prod}}{\bf 1}_{\{({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}},{\overline{X}}_{t+s_{i}})\in{\cal O}_{i}\}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\varphi)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t,\overline{\mu}}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\varphi)\big)\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\prod}}{\bf 1}_{\{({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}},{\overline{X}}_{t+s_{i}})\in{\cal O}_{i}\}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
So
{\overline{P}}
is also of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{3}_{t}
, which shows
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{1}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{2}_{t}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{3}_{t}
.

2)
Next, let
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{1}_{t,{\bf x}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{2}_{t}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{3}_{t}
.

2a)

Fix
i,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\{1,\cdots\negthinspace,d\}
. We set
\phi_{i}(w,x)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace w_{i}
and
\phi_{ij}(w,x)\negthinspace:=\negthinspace w_{i}w_{j}

for any
w\negthinspace=\negthinspace(w_{1},\cdots\negthinspace,w_{d})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
x\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Clearly,
\phi_{i},\phi_{ij}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d+l})
.
One can calculate that
{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t,i}_{s}
,
{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t,i}_{s}{\overline{W}}^{t,j}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\delta_{ij}(s\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)
,
where
{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{W}}^{t,1}_{s},\cdots\negthinspace,{\overline{W}}^{t,d}_{s}\big)
.

Let
n\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
and
(\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{Q}^{2,<}_{+}
. As
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{3}_{t}
,
it holds for any
\big\{(s_{i},{\cal O}_{i})\big\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big(\mathbb{Q}\cap(0,\mathfrak{s}]\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
that

E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{i})\big)\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\prod}}{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}}\in{\cal O}_{i}\}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0

and
E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{ij})\big)\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\prod}}{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}}\in{\cal O}_{i}\}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
So the Lambda-system
\overline{\Lambda}^{t,n}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r}}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big\{{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)\negthinspace:E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{i})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\hbox{ and }E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{ij})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\\
\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\Big\}

includes the Pi-system
\Big\{\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{k}{\cap}}({\overline{W}}^{t}_{t+s_{i}})^{-1}({\cal O}_{i})\Big)\negthinspace:\big\{(s_{i},{\cal O}_{i})\big\}^{k}_{i=1}\negthinspace\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\big(\mathbb{Q}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace(0,\mathfrak{s}]\big)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\Big\}
,
which generates
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}
.
Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem renders that
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\overline{\Lambda}^{t,n}_{\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{i})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\hbox{ and }E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{r})}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land(t+\mathfrak{s})}(\phi_{ij})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0,~\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t+\mathfrak{s}}.\quad

(6.19)

Let
t\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s\negthinspace<\negthinspace r\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
and
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{s}
.
Taking
(\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{r})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Big(\frac{\lceil(s-t)2^{m}\rceil}{2^{m}},\frac{1+\lceil(r-t)2^{m}\rceil}{2^{m}}\Big)
,
m\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
in (
6.19
)
and sending
m\negthinspace\to\negthinspace\infty
, we can deduce from
the continuity of bounded processes
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{i})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
and
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{ij})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

that
E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land r}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land s}(\phi_{i})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
and
E_{\overline{P}}\Big[\big({\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land r}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{M}}^{t}_{{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\land s}(\phi_{ij})\big){\bf 1}_{{\overline{A}}}\Big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0
.
So
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{i})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
and
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s\land{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}}(\phi_{ij})\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
are two
\big({\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}},{\overline{P}}\big)-
martingales.
As
\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\negthinspace\mathop{\uparrow}\,{\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\infty
,
we see that
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\phi_{i})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t,i}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

and
\big\{{\overline{M}}^{t}_{s}(\phi_{ij})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t,i}_{s}{\overline{W}}^{t,j}_{s}\negthinspace-\negthinspace\delta_{ij}(s\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
are
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}-
local martingales.
Lévy’s characterization theorem implies that
{\overline{W}}^{t}
is a Brownian motion on
\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}),{\overline{P}}\big)
.
So
{\overline{P}}
satisfies (D2) of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.
We still have (
6.18
) since
{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}
is also a Brownian motion under
{\overline{P}}
.

2b)
As
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal P}}}^{2}_{t}
, there exist a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
predictable process

\ddot{\nu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{\ddot{\nu}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

with all paths in
\mathbb{J}
and a
[0,\infty]-
valued
{\bf F}^{W,P_{0}}-
stopping time
\ddot{\gamma}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that

{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\Gamma\big(\ddot{\nu},\ddot{\gamma}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\big(W,\ddot{\nu},\ddot{\gamma}\big)^{-1}
.
Given
D\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{J}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{T})
, taking

in (
6.18
) yields that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}\big\{({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t},{\overline{T}}\negthinspace-\negthinspace t)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{Q}}_{t,{\overline{P}}}(D)\negthinspace=\negthinspace P_{0}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\big(W,\ddot{\nu},\ddot{\gamma}\big)^{-1}(D)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})^{-1}\big((W,\ddot{\nu},\ddot{\gamma})^{-1}(D)\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}),\ddot{\gamma}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace D\big\},

which shows the joint
{\overline{P}}-
distribution of
({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t},{\overline{T}})
is the same as the joint
{\overline{P}}-
distribution of
\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}),t\negthinspace+\negthinspace\ddot{\gamma}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)
.
Similar to Part (2b) in the proof of
[
4
, Proposition 4.1]
, we can use
the equality
{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{T}})^{-1}\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\negthinspace,t\negthinspace+\negthinspace\ddot{\gamma}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)^{\negthinspace-1}

to derive that
{\overline{P}}\{{\overline{T}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace t\negthinspace+\negthinspace\ddot{\gamma}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
. Namely,
{\overline{P}}
satisfies (D4
′
) or equivalently
(D4) of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
.

We claim that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{A}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{A}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}\big),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty},~\,\forall\,{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J}).

(6.20)

To see this, we fix
{\cal A}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{J})
and define
\Lambda\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{A}}\in\mathscr{B}_{\overline{P}}({\overline{\Omega}})\negthinspace:{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{A}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{A}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\big\{\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}\big)\big\}
.
As
{\overline{P}}\big\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}\\
\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\{({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\{({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}))\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big\{\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\big\}
, we see that
{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Lambda
and
\Lambda
is thus a Lambda- system.

For any
(\mathfrak{s},{\cal E})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, since
W_{\mathfrak{s}}\negthinspace:\Omega_{0}\negthinspace\mapsto\negthinspace\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a continuous function,
one has
W^{-1}_{\mathfrak{s}}({\cal E})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})
.
Then it holds for any
\{(\mathfrak{s}_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\}^{N}_{i=1}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}\Big(\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})\Big)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\}\Big)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\Big(\Big\{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}W^{-1}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}({\cal E}_{i})\Big\}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\}\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\Big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},{\overline{\mathscr{U}}}^{t}\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}W^{-1}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}({\cal E}_{i})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\Big\}

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\Big\{\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t},\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\big)\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}W^{-1}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}}({\cal E}_{i})\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\cal A}\Big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\Big(\Big(\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})\Big)\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal A}\}\Big).

So
\Lambda
contains the Pi-system
\Big\{\underset{i=1}{\overset{N}{\cap}}({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}_{\mathfrak{s}_{i}})^{-1}({\cal E}_{i})\negthinspace:(\mathfrak{s}_{i},{\cal E}_{i})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),i\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1,\cdots,N\Big\}
, which generates
{\cal F}^{{\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}}_{\infty}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\infty}
.
Dynkin’s Pi-Lambda Theorem implies that
{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace\Lambda
,
proving the claim (
6.20
).

Let
n,k,j\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathbb{N}
. By Lemma
A.3
,
{\cal O}_{n,k,j}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{\omega_{0}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\Omega_{0}\negthinspace:\ddot{\nu}_{\cdot}(\omega_{0})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{i}_{\overset{}{\mathbb{J}}}^{-1}\big(O_{\frac{1}{n}}(\mathfrak{m}_{k},\phi_{j})\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{W,P_{0}}_{\infty}
. Since (
6.18
) shows that

\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\big)^{-1}({\cal O}_{n,k,j})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}
, applying
(
6.20
) with
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big({\overline{\mathscr{W}}}^{t}\big)^{-1}({\cal O}^{c}_{n,k,j})
and

Proof of Proposition

:

,

1)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace:=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace(\varphi\negthinspace\circ\negthinspace\Xi)\big(t,\mathscr{T}_{n}\big(t,{\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace\land\negthinspace n\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\mathfrak{s},{\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\cal I}_{\varphi}\big(t,\mathscr{T}_{n}\big(t,{\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)\negthinspace\land\negthinspace n\negthinspace\land\negthinspace\mathfrak{s},{\overline{W}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{X}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{U}}({\overline{\omega}})\big)

(6.24)

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big({\overline{M}}^{t}(\varphi)\big)\big({\overline{\tau}}^{t}_{n}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\land\negthinspace(t\negthinspace+\negthinspace\mathfrak{s}),{\overline{\omega}}\big),\quad\,\forall\,(t,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}

is

.

Let

),

Proof of Proposition

:

\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace>\negthinspace t\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
.

Suppose first that
\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace t\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
: for any

,

{\overline{Y}}^{i}_{\negthinspace{\overline{P}}}(t)\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{{\overline{T}}\land t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big|{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{t}\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}g_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace y_{i}
and
{\overline{Z}}^{i}_{\overline{P}}(t)\negthinspace=\negthinspace E_{\overline{P}}\big[\int_{t}^{\overline{T}}h_{i}(r,{\overline{X}}_{r\land\cdot},{\overline{U}}_{r})dr\big]\negthinspace=\negthinspace z_{i}

Then

Let us assume
\big\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace>\negthinspace t\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}
in the rest of this proof.
By (D1) of
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}
, there exists a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{W^{t}}-
predictable process

\widehat{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\{\widehat{\mu}_{s}\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}
on
\Omega_{0}
such that
the
{\overline{P}}-
measure of
{\overline{\Omega}}_{\mu}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{U}}_{r}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{r}({\overline{W}})\hbox{ for a.e. }r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}

is equal to
1
.
Since
\overline{\mu}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}_{s}({\overline{W}})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty)

is a

satisfies that
for any

\displaystyle\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big({\overline{U}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\overline{\mu}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{J}}^{\,t}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}),\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,\infty).

(6.36)

Set

II.a.1)

by

\displaystyle\big({\overline{W}}^{t,{\overline{\gamma}}}_{r},{\overline{U}}^{t,{\overline{\gamma}}}_{r}\big)({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\Big({\overline{W}}^{t}\big((r\negthinspace\vee\negthinspace t)\negthinspace\land\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}),{\overline{\omega}}\big),{\bf 1}_{\{r\in[0,t)\cup({\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}),\infty)\}}u_{0}\negthinspace+\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{r\in[t,{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}})]\}}{\overline{U}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\Big),\quad\,\forall\,(r,{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}.

Clearly,

{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{\overline{\gamma}}\big/\mathscr{B}(\Omega_{0})-

To show the measurability of
{\overline{U}}^{t,{\overline{\gamma}}}

\varphi\negthinspace\in\negthinspace L^{0}\big((0,\infty)\negthinspace\times\negthinspace\mathbb{U};\mathbb{R}\big)

The

for any

Since
{\overline{\Omega}}_{\mu}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}({\overline{U}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}))dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\overline{\mu}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\big)dr,\,\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(t,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\underset{s\in\mathbb{Q}\in(t,\infty)}{\cap}\big\{{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\overline{\mu}_{r}({\overline{\omega}})\big)dr\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t},\overline{\Upsilon}^{t},{\overline{P}}}_{\infty}

1
with

{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{\,{\overline{\omega}}}_{\negthinspace X}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:{\overline{X}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\;\hbox{ for some }s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,\infty)\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}}\big({\overline{{\cal F}}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}_{\infty}\big)
, where

\Big\{\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}},{\overline{X}}_{{\overline{\gamma}}\land\cdot}({\overline{\omega}}),\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}}_{s}\Big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}

is an
\Big\{{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}},{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}}_{s\vee t_{\overline{\omega}}}\Big\}_{s\in[0,\infty)}-
adapted continuous process
that uniquely solves the following SDE with the open-loop control
\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}
on
\big({\overline{\Omega}},\mathscr{B}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big),{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}\big)
:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{X}}\big(t_{\overline{\omega}},{\overline{\omega}}\big)+\int_{t_{\overline{\omega}}}^{s}b\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{r}\big)dr\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{t_{\overline{\omega}}}^{s}\sigma\big(r,\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{r\land\cdot},\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{r}\big)d{\overline{W}}_{r},\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty)

with initial condition
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{X}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}})
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,t_{\overline{\omega}}]
.

Since
\big\{\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\widehat{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}({\overline{W}})\big\}_{s\in[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty)}

is a
\mathbb{U}-
valued,
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}}-
predictable process on
{\overline{\Omega}}
,
the
[0,1)-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}}-
adapted continuous process

{\overline{J}}^{\,{\overline{\omega}}}_{s}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\int_{t_{\overline{\omega}}}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}(\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{r})dr
,
\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty)
satisfies that
for any
{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\overline{\Upsilon}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t_{\overline{\omega}}}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big({\overline{U}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace\int_{t_{\overline{\omega}}}^{s}e^{-r}\mathscr{I}\big(\overline{\mu}^{\overline{\omega}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\big)dr\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{J}}^{\,{\overline{\omega}}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}),\quad\,\forall\,s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty).

(6.44)

Like Lemma 2.4 of
[
38
]
, one can construct an
\mathbb{R}^{l}-
valued
{\bf F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}}-
predictable process
\big\{{\overline{K}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty)}

such that
{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{\,{\overline{\omega}}}_{\negthinspace K}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:{\overline{K}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace\neq\negthinspace\overline{\mathscr{X}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})
for some
s\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t_{\overline{\omega}},\infty)\big\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{N}_{{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}}\big({\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}}_{\infty}\big)
.

II.b.3)
Let
{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}})
. We claim that

\displaystyle{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{A}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}\}}{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{A}}),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{\overline{\gamma}},~\,\forall\,{\overline{\omega}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}_{*}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}_{*}.

(6.45)

To see this, we take
{\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}}
.
Let
{\overline{\omega}}_{1}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}_{*}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}_{*}
and set
s_{1}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}_{1})
.
Since
{\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s_{1}\}
is an
{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{s_{1}}-
measurable set including
{\overline{\omega}}_{1}
,
one can deduce that

\displaystyle{\overline{\Theta}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}_{1}}

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace=\negthinspace

\displaystyle\negthinspace\negthinspace\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{r})({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{r})({\overline{\omega}}_{1}),\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s_{1}];

(6.46)

\displaystyle\qquad{\overline{X}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{X}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}_{1}),\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,s_{1}]\big\}\hbox{ is also contained in ${\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s_{1}\}$. }

By (
6.43
),
{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{1}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{{\cal A}}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1
and thus
{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{1}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{1}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\omega}}_{1}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}\}}{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{1}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{A}})
. We next let
{\overline{\omega}}_{2}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}_{*}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal N}}}^{c}_{*}
and set
s_{2}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}}_{2})
.
As
{\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s_{2}\}
is an
{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{s_{2}}-
measurable set including
{\overline{\omega}}_{2}
,

{\overline{\Theta}}^{t}_{{\overline{\gamma}},{\overline{\omega}}_{2}}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{\omega}}^{\prime}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{\Omega}}\negthinspace:({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{r})({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r},\overline{\Upsilon}^{t}_{r})({\overline{\omega}}_{2}),\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[t,s_{2}];\,{\overline{X}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{X}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}_{2}),\,\forall\,r\negthinspace\in\negthinspace[0,s_{2}]\big\}

is also included in
{\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s_{2}\}
.
We correspondingly have
{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{2}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c})\negthinspace=\negthinspace 1

and thus
{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{2}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace 0\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\omega}}_{2}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}\}}{\overline{Q}}^{{\overline{\omega}}_{2}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{A}})
.

Consider a pasted probability measure
{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathfrak{P}\big({\overline{\Omega}}\big)
:

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{A}})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c}_{*}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}_{*}}{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{A}}){\overline{P}}(d{\overline{\omega}}),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace\mathscr{B}({\overline{\Omega}}).

(6.47)

In particular, taking

in (
6.45
) renders that

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}({\overline{{\cal A}}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}\big({\overline{{\cal A}}}^{c}_{*}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal A}}}\big)\negthinspace+\negthinspace\int_{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}_{*}}{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\omega}}\in{\overline{{\cal A}}}\}}{\overline{P}}(d{\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}({\overline{{\cal A}}}),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{{\cal A}}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{\overline{\gamma}}.

(6.48)

In the next four parts, we demonstrate that
{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}
also belongs to
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
,
i.e., the probability class
{\overline{{\cal P}}}_{t,{\bf x}}(y,z)
is stable under the pasting (

).

II.c)

In this part, we demonstrate that
{\overline{W}}^{t}
is a Brownian motion
with respect to the filtration
{\overline{{\bf G}}}^{t}\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big\{{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{s}\big\}_{s\in[t,\infty)}

under
{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}
.
More precisely, we need to verify that for any
t\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s\negthinspace<\negthinspace r\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
and

\displaystyle{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}\big\{\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s}\big)^{-1}({\cal E})\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace{\overline{A}}\big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}\big\{\big({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s}\big)^{-1}({\cal E})\big\}{\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{A}}\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace\phi(r\negthinspace-\negthinspace s,{\cal E}){\overline{P}}_{\varepsilon}\big({\overline{A}}\big),\quad\,\forall\,{\overline{A}}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{s},

where
\phi(a,{\cal E})\negthinspace:=\negthinspace\big(2\pi a\big)^{-d/2}\int_{z\in{\cal E}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2a}}dz
,
\,\forall\,a\negthinspace\in\negthinspace(0,\infty)
.

II.c.1)
Let
t\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s\negthinspace<\negthinspace r\negthinspace<\negthinspace\infty
,

and

Since
\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace r\}\negthinspace\in\negthinspace{\cal F}^{{\overline{W}}^{t}}_{{\overline{\gamma}}\land r}\negthinspace\subset\negthinspace{\overline{{\cal G}}}^{t}_{\overline{\gamma}}
and

\displaystyle{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}})\geq r\}}{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}\Big(({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s})^{-1}({\cal E})\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}\Big(\{{\overline{\gamma}}\negthinspace\geq\negthinspace r\}\negthinspace\cap\negthinspace({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s})^{-1}({\cal E})\Big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\bf 1}_{\{{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}})\geq r\}\cap\big\{{\overline{\omega}}\in({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}-{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s})^{-1}({\cal E})\big\}}.

(6.49)

\bullet
If
{\overline{\gamma}}({\overline{\omega}})\negthinspace\leq\negthinspace s
, since
({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s})({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}_{r}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}_{s}({\overline{\omega}}^{\prime})

and since

under

by (

),

\displaystyle{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}\big(({\overline{W}}^{t}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t}_{s})^{-1}({\cal E})\big)\negthinspace=\negthinspace{\overline{Q}}^{\overline{\omega}}_{\varepsilon}\Big\{\Big({\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}_{r}\negthinspace-\negthinspace{\overline{W}}^{t_{\overline{\omega}}}_{s}\Big)^{-1}({\cal E})\Big\}\negthinspace=\negthinspace\phi(r\negthinspace-\negthinspace s,{\cal E}).

(6.50)

\bullet
We next suppose that

and
